# WIREFRAME — Master Chapter 4 Manuscript

**Vision-Based Diagnostic Framework for Urban Overhead Wire Infrastructure Risk Assessment**

Consolidated results chapter covering every development version from the v2.6 baseline to v2.13. Generated by
`scripts/build_master_manuscript.py`.

| Part | Contents |
|---|---|
| **A** | This synthesis: what was built, the final configuration, the headline numbers, every negative result, and how the test split was used. |
| **B** | Every versioned report, verbatim (v2.7 → v2.13). |
| **C** | Appendices: the complete disclosure log (v2.1 → v2.13), the shipped configuration, an index of all results files, the figure index with captions, the defense summary, the dataset source register and the git history. |

Every number in Part A is taken from Part B or C and names the section or file it comes from.

**Hardware.** Every training, evaluation and latency run reported here, v2.6 to v2.13, was executed locally on a single Apple Silicon M1 laptop with **8 GB of unified memory** (PyTorch MPS for the GPU; CPU and GPU share the 8 GB). No cloud or external GPU was used. (For v2.13 the unit tests, browser verification and documentation build were also run on a Windows 11 PC, CPU only; no Windows timing is reported as a latency figure.) The memory limit shaped the design: model size, batch size, one heavy process at a time, and the latency figures, which come from a fanless laptop that throttles under sustained load (v2.10 §4.12.3).

**What is claimed:** a working, explainable screening prototype, measured honestly on a 145-photo dataset.

**What is not claimed:** none of the Table 3.10 targets is met, and no result here validates the allowable sag
limit δ_lim, which is still a development placeholder awaiting the consulting engineer's reference ratings (H3).

---

# Part A — Synthesis

## A.1 System summary (final state, v2.13)

A street photo of a pole goes through the following stages.

1. **Ingestion** (v2.10.1). Every readable image is accepted and normalised to 8-bit RGB (EXIF rotation,
   16-bit, alpha, CMYK, palette) and capped at 2048 px. GPS is stripped at the JPEG-segment level, so the
   analysed pixels are the uploaded pixels. Tiny or blank photos are accepted and graded Retake.
2. **Pre-processing** (Algorithm 1). A 1024 px letterbox for segmentation. Measured, triggered enhancement:
   edge-preserving denoise, CLAHE for low contrast or glare (not for darkness, v2.8), and blur-adaptive unsharp
   masking. A separate 640 px grey path for the VNI, never enhanced (Section 3.6.2).
3. **Segmentation.**
   - **Vegetation** comes from YOLO11s-seg at 1024.
   - **Wires** come from **WireNet**, a full-resolution U-Net (ResNet-34, ImageNet-pretrained) trained with
     cross-entropy + Dice + clDice. It uses flip test-time augmentation (v2.8) and, for dark photos,
     dual-polarity inference (v2.11).
   - Optional (v2.12, not shipped): a three-model probability fusion of WireNet-R34 with MiT-B1 and ConvNeXt-Tiny
     U-Nets, selected by naming `models/v212_ensemble.json` in `segmentation.wirenet_weights`. More accurate on
     validation, but about 4.7 s per photo on the M1.
   - **Ground gate** (v2.13): a pretrained ADE20K scene parser (UperNet-ConvNeXt-T, MIT licence, 384 px,
     0.26 s on the M1) labels road, sidewalk, floor and other ground. A wire span lying ≥ 50% on ground is a kerb,
     manhole or pavement edge: it is returned in `suppressed_spans` with its reason and never scored. The parser is
     skipped when no span reaches the lower half of the frame.
   - YOLO-seg masks were abandoned for wires because their 1/4-resolution prototype grid caps a 2 px wire at a
     median IoU of 0.40 (v2.7 §4.3.1).
4. **Span assembly** (v2.7, v2.8): Zhang–Suen skeleton, junction splitting with a 5 px cluster radius,
   collinear re-joining at 35°, collinear gap bridging (70 px, 25°), minimum stitched length 90 px.
5. **Chapter III mathematics, unchanged**:
   - Zhang–Suen centreline with spur pruning (Algorithm 4);
   - Levenberg–Marquardt catenary fit y = a·cosh((x − b)/a) + c (Eq. 6);
   - sag ratio δ/L (Eq. 8); allowable sag δ_lim (Eq. 9);
   - CRS = min(10, 10·(δ/L)/δ_lim·w) (Eq. 10), banded Low < 3 ≤ Medium ≤ 6 < High (Table 1.2);
   - VNI from a Hann-windowed 2-D FFT, MSPI ranking.
   CRS is computed only for chords ≥ 100 px, the measurement-precision gate σ/L ≤ ¼·δ_lim (v2.8 §4.6.2).
6. **Interpretation layer**, which reads the CRS and changes none of it:
   - **The pole verdict** AT RISK – HIGH / MODERATE / NOT AT RISK / INCONCLUSIVE – RETAKE PHOTO. A sag band
     counts only when more spans are in it than the measured per-span test–retest false-alarm rate explains (a
     one-sided binomial test, p0 = 0.050 / 0.090, α = 0.05; v2.9). Vegetation contact rules are absolute (v2.8).
   - **The reliability grade** A / B / C / Retake, from R = 1 − (U + D)/2 (v2.8), with a plain-language cause
     (v2.9) and a photometric glare gate (v2.10).
7. **Outputs.**
   - The field screener (v2.11, redesigned in v2.13): a hero verdict card (HAZARD DETECTED / REVIEW REQUIRED /
     NOT AT RISK / RETAKE PHOTO, worst CRS, measured vs total spans, one next action), the photo with Original /
     Detected spans / Full wire mask views and an overlay opacity slider, every span with its reason and the action
     that would let it be measured (v2.13), and a collapsed diagnostics drawer.
   - The admin command centre.
   - A PDF report with the three-panel diagnostic figure.
   - A batch street-audit CLI with a LabelMe review queue.

## A.2 Data (v2.5, v2.7 §4.1)

- **Dataset:** Wireframes-Prelabelled-Datasets, a span-level polygon export; 145 photos, split 102 train /
  22 validation / 21 test.
- **Classes:** Vegetation, abandoned-wire, power-distribution-lines, telecom-cable.
- **External sources:** none passed the five-gate screen (polygon masks, ground-level viewpoint, span-level
  labels, allowed licence, mappable classes). The search was done in v2.7 and repeated in v2.11, which added
  WireSegHR (no licence), MPCD (aerial) and railway OCS (catenary).
- **Validation and test use:** validation decided every rule and constant. Test use is accounted for in A.6.

v2.13's single test run on the frozen pipeline (clean, low light, synthetic night) is **[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]**;
the gate removes no validation span, so it is expected to match the rows above unless a test photo contains ground
(v2.13 §4.15.11).

v2.12 ships v2.11's pipeline, so its single test run (clean, low light, synthetic night;
`v212_spans_test.jsonl`) reproduces the v2.11 rows above in every metric field (v2.12 §4.14.7). The more accurate
three-model fusion was never run on test (it was not shipped, on latency grounds), so its validation gain
(0.0397 → 0.0537) has no test confirmation yet.

**Validation, pixel level** (WireNet-R34, clean, 2 px tolerant): power IoU 0.528, F1 0.792; telecom IoU 0.422,
F1 0.677 (v2.12 baseline row).

**Pole verdicts, test**: high / moderate / not at risk 18 / 3 / 0 (v2.8) → 7 / 7 / 7 (v2.9 onwards). A pole reads
INCONCLUSIVE whenever the photo is graded Retake and no hazard was found.

**Latency**: 1.33–3.46 s per upload across the 14 server-check cases on a quiet machine (v2.10 §4.12.3, run A), 1.25–6.21 s under OS background load (run C, `server_check_v210.json`). The densest pole (81 spans) went from
4.58 s to 2.3 s in v2.10, with outputs proven identical. A dense pole can exceed 3.5 s under sustained load on this
fanless laptop. Dark photos pay two extra WireNet passes (v2.11). The v2.13 ground gate adds 0.26 s when it runs; a
first profile under load (validation median 1.73 → 2.29 s; night pole 2.29 → 4.23 s) showed jumps larger than that
cost, and the < 3.0 s target awaits a quiet re-measurement (**[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]**, v2.13 §4.15.10).

**A recurring finding.** Pixel accuracy and span accuracy have disagreed four times: YOLO masks (v2.7), the
motion router (v2.9), hysteresis (v2.11) and the single v2.12 backbones (span mAP50 up, telecom pixel F1 down). The
three-model fusion is the first change to raise both. The screener needs continuous, separated spans, so span-level
validation decides, with pixel F1 as a guard.

## A.6 Methodological integrity: how the test split was used

- **No rule, threshold or constant was chosen on test.** Every choice was made on the 22 validation photos.
- **The test split was evaluated more than once in v2.9.** A coupling bug (service drops counted as failed wires in
  the blur fallback) was found because a locked run differed from v2.8. p0 was then re-measured on validation over
  all spans. Every superseded run is kept in `results/superseded/` (v2.9 §4.11.6).
- **v2.10:** the gate was chosen on validation, and the test glare evaluation was run once. The motivating failure
  turned out to be verdict instability, not glare dropout. The exposure-only alternative that would have caught it
  was **not** adopted, because choosing it after a test failure would leak test information (v2.10 §4.12.4).
- **v2.11:** the test run was interrupted once and completed by a second process, which gave identical duplicate
  rows; these were removed (v2.11 §4.13.5).
- **v2.12:** backbones and fusion weights were selected on validation only (15 grid points with the mirrored pass,
  15 without). The gain was checked with a paired bootstrap (94.4% of resamples, interval includes zero), and the
  winner's score is called optimistic. The fusion was rejected on latency, so the frozen v2.12 equals v2.11. The test
  split was run once on that frozen pipeline; no fusion was ever run on test.
- **v2.13:** every pre-processing, bridging and span-rule candidate, and the gate threshold, were decided on
  validation. The gate's benefit was shown on unlabelled live photos, which are not part of any split. The test
  split is to be run once, after freezing (**[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]**).

## A.7 Open problems, in order of expected impact

1. **δ_lim calibration and reference ratings (H3).** Every sag band depends on the placeholder δ_lim = 0.040.
2. **Verdict instability.** The pole verdict changes in 41% of mildly degraded validation photos whose wires survive
   (v2.10 §4.12.4). A steadier per-span sag measurement is needed.
3. **Perspective.** Spans that recede in depth read high 3.3× more often (v2.12). Image-space sag is
   perspective-sensitive, and a metric-depth correction needs ground truth.
4. **Telecom recall.** Telecom recall at the operating threshold is 0.05 on test (0.025 at night).
5. **Real night data.** Night gains are measured on synthetic night photos, and YOLO vegetation is unvalidated at
   night.
6. **Abandoned wire.** 27 labelled instances are not enough to learn the class.
7. **Street-level false wires.** v2.13 removes traces on the road and pavement, but fence and railing traces in
   front of facades remain (no safe cue without retraining), and the gate's effect has no labelled street-level
   measurement yet.
8. **Glare and motion blur** remain the hardest capture conditions. The reliability grade and the glare gate turn
   them into "retake", not into results.
9. **Compute.** The more accurate fusion (v2.12) is blocked only by latency on an 8 GB M1. On faster hardware it is
   the first configuration to test, after re-checking p0 and the grade on validation and running test once.

##### 4.1.2 External datasets screened and rejected

The search covered Kaggle, Roboflow Universe, GitHub, Zenodo, Mendeley Data and IEEE DataPort, on
2026-11-14. A source had to pass five gates:
1. polygon instance masks
2. a ground-level viewpoint (§3.4.2 step 2)
3. span-level rather than fragment labels
4. an allowed licence
5. classes that map onto the four WIREFRAME classes

No external source passed. The full register is in `ml/sources.yaml` under `rejected:`.

| Dataset | Reason rejected |
|---|---|
| Rural distribution power line vegetation management (Zenodo 18392898, CC BY 4.0, 2,404 images) | Files restricted; access must be requested from the authors |
| SEwire "wire" (Roboflow, CC BY 4.0) | Not inspected: no Roboflow API key. Its classes (`Tangled-wires`, `Electrical-wires-across-trees`) do not distinguish power from telecom |
| TTPLA (GitHub) | Aerial drone viewpoint of transmission towers |
| VEPL (Zenodo 7800234) | Top-down orthomosaic tiles |
| WireSegHR (CVPR 2023) | A single generic wire class; no licence or download located |
| Electric Wires Dataset (Kaggle) | Synthetic chroma-key cable images |
| OHL-UK (BMVC 2025) | Labels poles and warning signs only |
| lonlonago LabelMe sets (GitHub) | Commercial listings; no licence |

The thesis manuscript cites no Kaggle dataset; every source in Table 3.4 is Roboflow Universe.

#### 4.2 Methods

##### 4.2.1 Models evaluated

| Model | Architecture | Input | Training |
|---|---|---|---|
| **v2.6 baseline** | YOLO11s-seg | 640 | 30 epochs on the earlier 99-image export (git tag `v2.6-baseline`) |
| **YOLO-1024** | YOLO11s-seg | 1024 | MPS, batch 1 with gradient accumulation to 64. 16 epochs, stopped early by patience 15 (best epoch 1, which is likely a fluke), then 11 more epochs from epoch 16; best is global epoch 22. Blur and contrast augmentations included |
| **WireNet** | U-Net with ResNet-34 encoder, 5 classes, full-resolution output | 1024 (512 tiles in training) | 40 epochs, 2.63 h on MPS; best epoch 29. Loss: class-weighted cross-entropy + soft Dice + clDice. Same blur and contrast augmentations |
| **Hybrid (deployed)** | YOLO-1024 supplies Vegetation; WireNet supplies wire pixels; spans are extracted from WireNet's class map (§4.2.2) | 1024 | – |

YOLO11m-seg could not be trained on this Mac. It ran out of unified memory (8 GB) at batch 4 and
640 px.

##### 4.2.2 Span extraction from WireNet (`app/pipeline/wire_instances.py`)

1. For each wire class: take the class mask, drop components under 30 px, then thin it with
   Zhang-Suen.
2. Cut the skeleton at junction pixels. Junction points within 10 px count as one crossing, since a
   shallow crossing thins into two junction points joined by a short bridge.
3. Re-join pieces that meet at a crossing when their end directions are collinear within 25°. A wire
   continues straight through a crossing.
4. Assign each mask pixel to its nearest piece using a Euclidean distance transform.
5. A span's confidence is the mean class probability over its pixels. Spans shorter than 30 px are
   dropped.

##### 4.2.3 Metrics

- **Strict (Equations 5 and 12–14):** an instance counts as a true positive at mask IoU ≥ 0.5,
  matched per class. AP is the 101-point COCO interpolation. P, R and F1 are reported at the operating
  threshold, `conf_min` = 0.20, which was chosen on the validation split by sweeping 0.05–0.75 for the
  best strict mean F1.
- **Tolerant (supplementary; not Equation 5):** both masks are widened by 2 px (at 1024 px) before
  computing IoU. On a 2 px wire, a one-pixel offset alone caps strict IoU at about 0.33.
- **Ultralytics validation:** box and mask metrics computed on the 1/4-resolution mask grid, with
  NMS IoU 0.45 and retina masks.
- **Degraded test:** every test image receives one blur, chosen deterministically from its file name.
  The blur is motion (1.2% of the long side), defocus (radius 0.4%) or Gaussian (σ 0.3%). On top of
  that, contrast is scaled by 0.50–0.65 and brightness shifted by ±20 (`ml/degrade.py`).
- **AP floor:** predictions under confidence 0.01 are excluded from AP. This is recorded in every
  result.

---

#### 4.3 Results

##### 4.3.1 Why YOLO-seg masks cannot represent wires

The question was what IoU a *perfect* mask on YOLO's 1/4-resolution grid would reach, once
upsampled and compared with the full-resolution label. Measured on 2,129 labelled instances:

| Class | Median width at 1024 | Ceiling IoU (median) | Instances able to reach IoU ≥ 0.5 |
|---|---|---|---|
| power-distribution-lines | 2.1 px | 0.40 | 17% |
| telecom-cable | 2.1 px | 0.41 | 21% |
| Vegetation | 7.1 px | 0.72 | 76% |
| abandoned-wire | 8.7 px | 0.78 | 67% |

So for the two main wire classes, YOLO-seg's mask AP50 is capped at roughly 0.2, however well the
model is trained. This matches the observed result: Ultralytics mask AP50 for power and telecom is
0.000 for both YOLO models (§4.3.4).

##### 4.3.2 SAM 2.1 prompted with the true boxes (upper bound for a "detector → SAM" pipeline)

Tested on 6 validation images. "Box + points" adds 3 points sampled along each labelled skeleton.

| Prompt | Class | n | Median IoU | IoU ≥ 0.5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| box | power-distribution-lines | 58 | 0.08 | 2% |
| box | telecom-cable | 62 | 0.04 | 0% |
| box + points | power-distribution-lines | 58 | 0.07 | 2% |
| box + points | telecom-cable | 63 | 0.04 | 0% |
| box + points | Vegetation | 26 | 0.52 | 58% |
| box + points | abandoned-wire | 3 | 0.65 | 100% |

Even with perfect prompts, SAM 2.1 does not segment 2 px wires. It also took about 40 s per image
on the CPU.

##### 4.3.3 WireNet pixel-level segmentation (validation, 22 images, best epoch 29)

| Class | Pixel IoU | Tolerant pixel IoU (2 px) |
|---|---|---|
| power-distribution-lines | 0.265 | 0.525 |
| telecom-cable | 0.232 | 0.418 |
| Vegetation | 0.204 | 0.203 |
| abandoned-wire | 0.036 | 0.045 |

##### 4.3.4 Instance segmentation on the held-out test split (21 images)

**Overall:**

| Model | Strict mask mAP50 | Strict mAP50-95 | Tolerant mAP50 |
|---|---|---|---|
| v2.6 baseline (640) | 0.0005 | 0.0001 | 0.0005 |
| YOLO-1024 | 0.0198 | 0.0042 | 0.0199 |
| **Hybrid (deployed)** | **0.0204** | **0.0043** | **0.0375** |

**Per class, hybrid** (P, R and F1 at `conf_min` 0.20):

| Class | Labelled | Predicted | Strict P / R / F1 / AP50 | Tolerant P / R / F1 / AP50 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| power-distribution-lines | 253 | 466 | 0.017 / 0.032 / 0.022 / 0.002 | 0.101 / 0.186 / 0.131 / 0.067 |
| telecom-cable | 361 | 910 | 0.004 / 0.011 / 0.006 / 0.000 | 0.020 / 0.050 / 0.028 / 0.004 |
| Vegetation | 38 | 11 | 0.364 / 0.105 / 0.163 / 0.079 | same |
| abandoned-wire | 7 | 0 | – / 0 / – / 0 | – |

At the operating threshold, YOLO-1024 alone predicts **0 power spans and 4 telecom spans** against
253 and 361 labelled. Its wire confidences sit almost entirely below 0.20. On validation, taking
spans from WireNet raised tolerant power-line matches from 0 to 52, and telecom from 0 to 25
(`v27_val_span_modes.json`).

**Ultralytics validation (box and mask, 1/4-resolution grid), standard test:**

| Model | Box mAP50 | Mask mAP50 | Power box AP50 | Telecom box AP50 | Vegetation mask AP50 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| v2.6 (640) | 0.102 | 0.003 | 0.202 | 0.177 | 0.014 |
| YOLO-1024 | 0.081 | 0.014 | 0.132 | 0.173 | 0.055 |

Wire mask AP50 is 0.000 for both models, as the §4.3.1 ceiling predicts.

##### 4.3.5 Blurred and low-contrast photos

| Model | Standard test | Degraded test | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| v2.6 (Ultralytics box mAP50) | 0.102 | 0.074 | **−28%** |
| YOLO-1024, trained with blur augmentations (box mAP50) | 0.081 | 0.090 | **+10% (no loss)** |
| v2.6 (Ultralytics mask mAP50) | 0.0034 | 0.0006 | −82% |
| YOLO-1024 (mask mAP50) | 0.0136 | 0.0132 | −3% |
| Hybrid, tolerant mAP50 | 0.0375 | 0.0124 | −67% |

**Effect of the inference-time enhancement on degraded photos** (hybrid; CLAHE on L* plus
blur-adaptive unsharp masking):

| Hybrid, degraded test | Strict mAP50 | Strict mAP50-95 | Tolerant mAP50 | Tolerant power F1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Enhancement off | 0.0051 | 0.0008 | 0.0104 | 0.076 |
| Enhancement on | **0.0079** | **0.0025** | **0.0124** | 0.057 |

Enhancement raises strict mAP50 by 55% and tolerant mAP50 by 19% on degraded photos. Tolerant
power-line F1 at the fixed threshold falls, because enhancement produces more candidate spans
(523 against 460), which lowers precision.

The blur fallback (a second pass with stronger enhancement and a mirrored copy) fired on 20 of 21
degraded photos with the YOLO-only models, and its result was kept 8 times (v2.6) and 14 times
(YOLO-1024). It never fired with the hybrid: WireNet still returns enough scored spans on degraded
photos, so the trigger condition (no spans, or half or more unscored) is not met.

**A bug found by this work:** with a fixed 1 px radius and a detail threshold of 4 grey levels,
unsharp masking returned a strongly blurred photo unchanged (sharpness gain 1.0 at σ 5). Blur-adaptive
radius and threshold restored the gain to 1.7–2.1 (`tests/test_blur_resilience.py`).

##### 4.3.6 Abandoned-wire

The model cannot learn abandoned-wire from the available labels.

**1. Too few examples, and some of them wrong.**
- **Counts:** 27 instances in all — 14 in training (7 photos), 6 in validation (3 photos) and 7 in
  test (2 photos). That is 0.033% of training pixels.
- **Label quality:** a visual audit of all 27 polygons found some that trace a door edge on a wall,
  a blob on a blurred pole, or a whole hanging bundle or the sky around it, rather than the wire. Only
  about a third are tight wire traces. Automatic tightening (below) set 9 of the 23 connected label
  components to *ignore*.

**2. What was tried.** Each attempt branched from the WireNet best checkpoint, with a guard keeping
power and telecom at ≥ 95% of their Stage 1 IoU.
- **Label tightening** to wire-like pixels, using the Stage 1 wire map plus a dark-ridge filter.
- **Oversampling:** 35% of tiles centred on abandoned-wire. The abandoned-wire pixel share rose from
  0.03% to 0.23%.
- **Copy-paste** of the tightened real instances, and **procedural hanging-end wires**.
- **Loss:** focal cross-entropy, Dice and clDice. One attempt also used logit adjustment (Menon et
  al., 2021).

**3. Outcome.** Abandoned-wire coverage on validation, where an instance counts as hit when
predictions run along ≥ 50% of its length, and precision is the share of predicted pixels within
6 px of a label:

| Model | Instances hit | Precision | Pixels predicted abandoned |
|---|---|---|---|
| WireNet Stage 1 | 1 / 6 | 0.25 | 3,360 (all 22 images) |
| Stage 2, weight 15 + logit adjustment, epoch 3 | 6 / 6 | 0.01 | 1,387,708 |
| Same, bias −4 at inference | 3 / 6 | 0.02 | 315,688 |
| Same, bias −5 | 0 / 6 | 0.01 | 49,191 |
| Stage 2, weight 6, epoch 1 | 4 / 6 | 0.04 | 100,215 |

Every rebalancing raised recall only by labelling ordinary wires as abandoned. At no inference bias
does precision exceed 0.02, so the model has learned no localised cue for abandoned-wire. That is
expected: an abandoned wire looks like any other wire. It differs by context — a free end, slack,
coiling, no termination — and 14 examples, about a third of them mislabelled, do not contain enough
of that context to learn. Oversampling, copy-paste and synthesis repeat the same 14 examples; they
cannot add the missing variety.

**Requirement:** several hundred tightly traced abandoned-wire instances, from a few hundred
different photos, before this class can be learned. Until then, the deployed model uses the Stage 1
weights, and any abandoned-wire output should be treated as unreliable.

##### 4.3.7 Downstream: catenary risk scores

On the end-to-end server check (8 uploads: 4 test photos, standard and degraded; 1.4–3.9 s each; PDF
reports valid), the hybrid produced 411 spans, of which 306 were scored:

| Risk level | Spans |
|---|---|
| Low (CRS < 3) | 216 |
| Medium (3–6) | 40 |
| High (> 6) | 50 (19 saturated at 10) |

The mean CRS was 2.34. Every photo's worst span was high, driven by short span fragments with chords
of 22–48 px, where a pixel of noise is a large share of the sag. Every score carries
`development_limits`, because δ_lim,0 = 0.040 is a placeholder.

---

#### 4.4 Limitations

1. **The dataset is small** (145 photos) and has a single source. Every figure has wide uncertainty:
   the test set is 21 images with 7 abandoned-wire instances.
2. **The strict instance metric is mismatched with the data.** Equation 5 at IoU 0.5, applied to
   2 px wires drawn by hand to ±1–2 px, rejects most correct spans. The tolerant metric is reported
   alongside it, and its definition is given in §4.2.3.
3. **Spans are fragmented.** WireNet spans break wherever the class mask has gaps: 466 predicted
   against 253 labelled for power, and 910 against 361 for telecom. This lowers precision and makes
   the per-photo maximum CRS noisy.
4. **The YOLO-1024 detector is under-trained.** Its best checkpoint, global epoch 22, came from a run
   cut short by early stopping on a fluke epoch-1 best.
5. **CRS, VNI and the engineering parameters are provisional** (Table 3.10 targets not evaluated: no
   engineer reference ratings yet).

#### 4.5 Recommended next steps, in order of expected impact

1. **Labels:** tightly trace several hundred abandoned-wire instances, and re-trace a sample of
   power and telecom spans, to measure the annotator noise floor.
2. **Span assembly:** bridge gaps along the skeleton direction when joining pieces, and require a
   minimum chord length before a span is scored.
3. **Retrain** WireNet and the detector on the larger set, and report strict and tolerant metrics on a
   test split of at least 100 photos.
4. **Agree** a tolerance-aware instance metric for thin structures with the panel, alongside
   Equation 5.


---

## B.2 Report v2.8 (`docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.8_autonomous_upgrades.md`)

### Chapter 4 (continued) — v2.8: Span Assembly, Hazard Interpretation, and Robustness

Branch `v2.8-overnight-sprint`, built from `v2.7` (`7867d56`). The v2.7 weights
(`models/wirenet-r34.pt`, SHA-256 prefix `ab2353c7`) and the YOLO detector were not retrained or
modified. Every change in this chapter is in pre-processing, test-time inference, span assembly or
interpretation. Every constant was chosen on the **validation split** (22 images). The **test
split** (21 images) was used once, for the final tables. Raw results are in
`docs/thesis/results/v28_*`; figures are in `docs/thesis/figures/`.

**Guard rule (set by the team).** A change is kept only if validation tolerant pixel IoU for
power-distribution-lines and telecom-cable stays ≥ 97% of v2.7's (0.525 and 0.418). Every accepted
change passed it (§4.8.4).

---

#### 4.6 Methods

##### 4.6.1 Span stitching (collinear gap bridging)

v2.7 split WireNet's class map into spans at skeleton junctions (§4.2.2). Spans still broke wherever
the mask had a gap — under occlusion, against low contrast, or across mask dropouts. On validation,
v2.7 produced 579 power spans for 314 labelled spans.

v2.8 joins spans *after* junction splitting. For each span, the skeleton is ordered along its
principal axis (first SVD component). Its two endpoints e₁ and e₂ get outward unit tangents t₁ and
t₂, each estimated from the mean of the 12 skeleton points nearest the end. Two spans of the same
class are joined through endpoints *a* and *b* when all three conditions hold:

    ‖b − a‖ ≤ g_max,    −t_a · t_b ≥ cos θ_max,    t_a · û ≥ cos θ_max  and  t_b · (−û) ≥ cos θ_max,
    with û = (b − a) / ‖b − a‖.

In words: the ends are close, the wires face each other, and the gap continues the wire's direction.
Candidate joins are taken nearest-first with union-find, and each endpoint is used at most once. The
bridge segment is drawn into the mask, 2 px wide. A span's confidence is the area-weighted mean of its
pieces' confidences. Finally, spans whose skeleton is shorter than L_stitch are dropped.

##### 4.6.2 Measurement-precision gate for CRS

The sag ratio δ/L is measured in pixels. If the skeleton has noise σ (about 1 px), the error in δ/L
is about σ/L. A CRS is meaningful only when that error is small relative to the allowable sag ratio
δ_lim. Requiring σ/L ≤ f·δ_lim gives

    L_min = σ / (f · δ_lim) = 1 px / (0.25 × 0.040) = 100 px.

Spans with chord L < 100 px get Chapter III's existing status `too_short`, with reason
`chord_below_precision_gate`, and no CRS. The gate follows from measurement precision. It was **not**
tuned to change the verdict distribution. Equation 10 is unchanged.

##### 4.6.3 Explainable hazard verdict (`app/pipeline/hazard.py`)

This is an interpretation layer on top of CRS; Equations 1–11 are unchanged. The verdict is the
highest severity raised by these rules (the vegetation rules were set by the team, not tuned):

| Factor | Rule | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Sag (Chapter III) | Any span with CRS level High (CRS > 6) | AT RISK – HIGH |
| | Any span with level Medium | AT RISK – MODERATE |
| Vegetation | A power span at distance 0 from a vegetation mask (contact) | AT RISK – HIGH |
| | A power span within 15 px | AT RISK – MODERATE |
| | A telecom span in contact | AT RISK – MODERATE |
| Congestion | Spans and junction crossings per megapixel | Informational only (no reference rating to calibrate against) |

Distances come from the Euclidean distance transform of the vegetation union, and the nearest wire
and vegetation points are recorded for the diagnostic figure.

**Approximate clearance.** With D the conductor diameter (config: 10.1 mm) and w̃ the median
measured power-wire width in pixels (area divided by skeleton length), the scale is
s = D / w̃ metres per pixel. The ±1 px width uncertainty is carried through to a range,
[D / (w̃ + 1), D / (w̃ − 1)]. At w̃ ≈ 2–3 px that range spans a factor of 2–3, so clearances are always
labelled approximate.

**Reliability override.** When the reliability grade (§4.6.6) is *Retake recommended*, NOT AT RISK becomes
**INCONCLUSIVE – RETAKE PHOTO**: an unreadable photo is never reported as safe.

##### 4.6.4 Topological loose-end detector (`app/pipeline/loose_ends.py`)

This detector targets abandoned wire using geometry alone, with no labels needed to run it. A span
endpoint is a candidate when all of these hold:
1. it is ≥ 12 px from the border and from letterbox padding;
2. no other wire lies within 8 px;
3. fewer than 2 other span endpoints lie within 25 px (an attachment proxy; the model has no pole
   class).

Score = 0.5 · downward + 0.5 · isolation.

##### 4.6.5 Pre-processing candidates and test-time augmentation

- **Multi-scale Retinex** on CIELAB L\* (Jobson et al., 1997). Scales are 1, 5 and 15% of the long
  side; the output is stretched at the 1st–99th percentiles and blended 0.6 with the original.
  Triggered when mean L\* ≤ 40 or when ≥ 15% of pixels are clipped high.
- **Edge-preserving denoise:** a bilateral filter (d = 5, σ_space = 3), triggered by moderate noise
  (Immerkær σ ≥ 3) or by JPEG blocking.
- **JPEG blockiness B:** the mean absolute step across 8-px block boundaries divided by the mean
  step inside blocks. B ≈ 1.0 for natural images and 1.4–1.7 at JPEG quality 20; the trigger is
  B ≥ 1.3.
- **Darkness trigger for CLAHE:** `dark_l_max`, tested on and off.
- **WireNet test-time augmentation:** `flip` averages the plain and mirrored probability maps.
  `flip_ms` adds 0.85× and 1.15× scales, each also mirrored.

##### 4.6.6 Reliability grade (`app/pipeline/quality.py`)

Two uncertainty measures are taken over pixels predicted as wire:
- **U:** the mean normalised entropy H(p) / log C.
- **D:** the share of those pixels whose class changes between the plain and mirrored passes.

Reliability is **R = 1 − (U + D) / 2**. The grade is A / B / C / *Retake recommended*, using
thresholds on R calibrated on validation (§4.8.5). A photo with fewer than 500 predicted wire pixels,
mean L\* < 8 or more than 85% clipped pixels is graded *Retake recommended* regardless of R.

##### 4.6.7 Stress conditions (`ml/stress.py`)

Each condition is deterministic per image name and scaled to the image size:

| Condition | Model |
|---|---|
| clean | unchanged |
| lowlight | γ = 2.4, plus Gaussian sensor noise σ = 6 |
| glare | ×1.6 + 60, with local contrast halved (Gaussian base, σ = 2% of the long side) |
| motion | linear kernel of 1.5% of the long side, random angle |
| defocus | disk kernel, radius 0.5% of the long side |
| jpeg | JPEG quality 20 |
| lowres | downscaled to 480 px on the long side, then upscaled back |
| rainhaze | haze (airlight 220, α = 0.35) plus slanted rain streaks |

Figure: `docs/thesis/figures/stress_conditions.jpg`.

---

#### 4.7 Experimental protocol

1. WireNet probabilities and YOLO vegetation detections were cached once per image (`ml/experiments/cache.py`).
   Span experiments re-use the cache, so every variant is scored on identical model outputs.
2. **Span constants** were chosen by coordinate descent on the validation tolerant mAP50, with two
   rounds and seven parameters, starting from v2.7's values. Parameters that ended at the edge of
   their grid were then extended (`v28_span_sweep.jsonl`, 64 runs).
3. **Pre-processing and TTA** were chosen on the validation stress set: 8 conditions × 22 images,
   scored by tolerant pixel IoU for power and telecom (`v28_stress_pixel_val.jsonl`). Combined
   candidates were then confirmed at span level.
4. **Decision rule:** differences below about 0.002 tolerant mAP50 were treated as ties. On 22 images
   the response is visibly noisy (§4.8.1), and among tied values the more conservative one was kept.
5. **Test split:** evaluated once, with the final configuration (§4.8.6–4.8.7).

---

#### 4.8 Results

##### 4.8.1 Span-extraction ablation (validation; v2.7 probability maps)

Each row varies one constant from the final setting.

| Varied constant (others at final) | Tolerant mAP50 at each value (final value in bold) |
|---|---|
| `max_gap` | 0: 0.0307, 35: 0.0339, 50: 0.0346, **70: 0.0356**, 100: 0.0330, 150: 0.0321 |
| `gap_angle_deg` | 10: 0.0336, 15: 0.0332, 20: 0.0328, **25: 0.0356**, 30: 0.0339, 35: 0.0366 |
| `draw_bridge` | False: 0.0350, **True: 0.0356** |
| `min_span_len` | 0: 0.0343, 45: 0.0345, 60: 0.0346, **90: 0.0356**, 120: 0.0354, 200: 0.0366 |
| `max_turn_deg` | 15: 0.0328, 25: 0.0350, **35: 0.0356**, 45: 0.0357 |
| `cluster_radius` | 2: 0.0262, **5: 0.0356**, 10: 0.0341, 15: 0.0289 |
| `min_len` | **15: 0.0356**, 30: 0.0340, 45: 0.0339 |

Final setting: gap bridging 70 px at 25°, bridges drawn, minimum stitched span 90 px, junction tolerance 35°, junction
cluster radius 5 px, minimum piece length 15 px. Full log: `v28_span_ablation_final.jsonl`; the descent log is in
`v28_span_sweep.jsonl`, 64 runs.

**Reading the table.**
- **Two effects are clear:** a sharp optimum in the junction cluster radius (2 px: 0.0262; 15 px: 0.0289) and a single
  peak in the bridging distance at 70 px (shorter bridges leave spans broken; longer ones join unrelated wires).
- **The angle and length effects are non-monotonic and within about ±0.002 (noise on 22 images).** Among those values,
  the stricter angle (25°) and the least restrictive length (90 px) were kept.

**Confirmation on the v2.8 probability maps** (edge denoise + flip TTA), validation:

| Span constants | Strict mAP50 | Tolerant mAP50 | Power tol. AP50 / F1 / R | Power spans (314 labelled) | Telecom tol. F1 / R |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| v2.7 | 0.0064 | 0.0296 | 0.068 / 0.125 / 0.175 | 564 | 0.043 / 0.066 |
| **v2.8** | **0.0068** | **0.0397** | **0.108 / 0.190 / 0.213** | **390** | **0.075 / 0.088** |

Together, v2.8's span assembly and pre-processing raise validation tolerant mAP50 from 0.0283 to
0.0397 (+40%). Fragmentation drops, from 1.84 to 1.24 predicted power spans per labelled span.

##### 4.8.2 The "always high" diagnosis

Worst CRS level per photo:

| | Validation (22): high / medium / low | Test (21): high / medium / low | High-band spans (val / test) |
|---|---|---|---|
| v2.7 behaviour (no stitching, no chord gate) | 19 / 1 / 2 | 21 / 0 / 0 | 90 / 120 |
| v2.8 | 17 / 3 / 2 | 18 / 2 / 1 | 46 / 75 |

What drives the v2.8 verdicts (validation / test):

| Driver | Validation | Test |
|---|---|---|
| Sag (high band) only | 13 | 14 |
| Sag (high) + telecom in contact with vegetation | 4 | 4 |
| Sag (medium), with or without telecom vegetation contact | 3 | 2 |
| Telecom vegetation contact only | 0 | 1 |
| Nothing (NOT AT RISK) | 2 | 0 |

Stitching and the chord gate halved the high-band spans, but most photos still read high. Three
causes, measured:

1. **The per-photo rule saturates.** On validation, 6.9% of 666 scored spans fall in the high band.
   A pole carries about 30 measurable spans, so the chance that at least one is high is
   1 − 0.931³⁰ ≈ 0.88.
2. **The placeholder δ_lim.** The high band (CRS > 6) means δ/L > 0.6 δ_lim = 0.024 at the
   placeholder δ_lim = 0.040. Measured image-space sag ratios: median 0.0017, 90th percentile 0.020,
   95th 0.028. Sag ratio is uncorrelated with chord length (Spearman ρ = −0.03), so the remaining
   tail is not short-span noise.

   Sensitivity (validation spans; this is evidence for calibration, not a choice):

   | δ_lim | Spans high / medium / low | P(≥ 1 high among 30 spans) |
   |---|---|---|
   | 0.040 (placeholder) | 0.069 / 0.093 / 0.838 | 0.88 |
   | 0.060 | 0.035 / 0.075 / 0.890 | 0.65 |
   | 0.080 | 0.023 / 0.047 / 0.931 | 0.50 |

3. **What the high spans are.** A visual audit (`figures/high_crs_spans_val.jpg`) found a mix. Some
   are genuinely strongly curved wires — mostly service drops and slack leads curving into a pole,
   which are correct fits but not span sag. Others are merge artefacts, where a span mask picked up a
   neighbouring bundle wire and the fit bent to follow it.

**Conclusion.** v2.8 removes the fragment-noise share of the problem. The remaining "high" rate
reflects three things: the placeholder δ_lim (whose calibration needs the engineer's reference
ratings, H3), a per-photo *worst-of-30* rule, and Chapter III's CRS not separating drop and
service wires from spans. None of these thresholds was tuned here. The per-photo aggregation rule —
for example, requiring k high spans, or scoring only pole-to-pole spans — is a methodology decision
for the team.

##### 4.8.3 Pre-processing and TTA under the eight conditions (validation, tolerant pixel IoU, power / telecom)

| Condition | Enhancement off | v2.7 | Edge denoise | v2.7 + flip | **v2.8** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| clean | 0.525 / 0.418 | 0.525 / 0.417 | 0.525 / 0.417 | 0.528 / 0.422 | **0.528 / 0.422** |
| lowlight | 0.504 / 0.360 | 0.464 / 0.334 | 0.494 / 0.345 | 0.472 / 0.345 | **0.512 / 0.368** |
| glare | 0.069 / 0.161 | 0.080 / 0.191 | 0.080 / 0.191 | 0.079 / 0.185 | 0.079 / 0.185 |
| motion | **0.375** / 0.271 | 0.360 / 0.270 | 0.360 / 0.270 | 0.357 / 0.277 | 0.357 / 0.277 |
| defocus | 0.387 / 0.290 | 0.395 / 0.310 | 0.395 / 0.310 | 0.403 / 0.314 | **0.403 / 0.314** |
| jpeg | 0.479 / 0.392 | 0.477 / 0.388 | 0.486 / 0.395 | 0.482 / 0.394 | **0.490 / 0.400** |
| lowres | 0.515 / 0.407 | 0.515 / 0.404 | 0.515 / 0.404 | 0.517 / 0.408 | **0.517 / 0.408** |
| rainhaze | 0.517 / 0.415 | 0.520 / 0.410 | 0.520 / 0.410 | 0.523 / 0.412 | **0.523 / 0.412** |
| **mean** | 0.3802 | 0.3788 | 0.3824 | 0.3824 | **0.3872** |

v2.8 = edge denoise + CLAHE darkness trigger off + flip TTA.

Further findings:
- **Retinex was rejected.** It lowered low-light power accuracy (0.453 against v2.7's 0.464) and
  glare telecom (0.151 against 0.191).
- **Multi-scale TTA was rejected.** It gained ≤ 0.006 over flip at 2–3.5 s per image, against
  0.5–0.9 s.
- **v2.7's darkness-triggered CLAHE was harmful in low light:** 0.464 against 0.504 with enhancement
  off. Removing that trigger alone gives 0.512 against 0.497 (v2.8 against v2.8-keepdark).
- **Edge denoise** fired only on JPEG and noisy low-light photos.
- **Glare stays catastrophic for every variant** (power ≈ 0.08): with the sky clipped to white,
  dark-wire contrast is gone. No preprocessing fix exists; recapture is needed, and the reliability
  grade flags 9 of 22 glare photos as *Retake recommended* (§4.8.5).
- **Motion blur:** the unsharp mask slightly hurts motion blur (0.360 against 0.375 off) while
  helping defocus. Telling motion blur from defocus is left as future work.

##### 4.8.4 Guard check

On clean validation, v2.8 scores tolerant pixel IoU of 0.528 for power (v2.7: 0.525, threshold
0.509) and 0.422 for telecom (v2.7: 0.418, threshold 0.405). **Pass.** The WireNet weights are
byte-identical to v2.7's.

##### 4.8.5 Reliability grade calibration (validation, 176 image × condition cases)

| Score | Spearman ρ with per-image tolerant IoU | p |
|---|---|---|
| R = 1 − (U + D) / 2 | **0.640** | 1.1 × 10⁻²¹ |
| −U (entropy only) | 0.551 | 2.4 × 10⁻¹⁵ |
| −D (flip disagreement only) | 0.594 | 3.9 × 10⁻¹⁸ |

The thresholds were chosen to maximise agreement with the measured accuracy bands (A ≥ 0.45,
B 0.30–0.45, C 0.15–0.30, Retake < 0.15): **A ≥ 0.78, B ≥ 0.66, C ≥ 0.64**. The grade matches the
measured band exactly in 63.1% of cases and within one band in 93.8%.

| Grade | n | Measured tolerant IoU (median) |
|---|---|---|
| A | 41 | 0.460 |
| B | 112 | 0.391 |
| C | 7 | 0.283 |
| Retake recommended | 16 | 0.093 |

Retake fires on 9 of 22 glare photos, 5 of 22 motion-blur photos and none of the clean photos.

##### 4.8.6 Test split: eight-condition stress benchmark (span level, full screener pipeline)

| Condition | Enhancement off (strict / tol.) | v2.7 (strict / tol.) | **v2.8** (strict / tol.) | v2.8 power tol. AP50 / R | v2.8 s/img* |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| clean | 0.0204 / 0.0376 | 0.0204 / 0.0372 | **0.0204 / 0.0469** | 0.105 / 0.225 | 5.47 |
| lowlight | 0.0011 / 0.0135 | 0.0018 / 0.0123 | **0.0053 / 0.0222** | 0.065 / 0.170 | 5.37 |
| glare | 0.0131 / 0.0156 | 0.0225 / 0.0281 | **0.0206 / 0.0257** | 0.019 / 0.043 | 4.73 |
| motion | 0.0215 / 0.0272 | 0.0158 / 0.0164 | **0.0166 / 0.0227** | 0.022 / 0.091 | 5.47 |
| defocus | 0.0220 / 0.0226 | 0.0257 / 0.0264 | **0.0255 / 0.0271** | 0.005 / 0.032 | 4.92 |
| jpeg | 0.0250 / 0.0405 | 0.0250 / 0.0401 | **0.0210 / 0.0410** | 0.078 / 0.186 | 4.34 |
| lowres | 0.0210 / 0.0374 | 0.0236 / 0.0377 | **0.0225 / 0.0503** | 0.105 / 0.209 | 4.4 |
| rainhaze | 0.0229 / 0.0400 | 0.0220 / 0.0399 | **0.0220 / 0.0520** | 0.117 / 0.237 | 5.0 |
| **mean** | 0.0184 / 0.0293 | 0.0196 / 0.0298 | **0.0192 / 0.0360** | | |

\* Seconds per image include the evaluation (mask matching). Server latency is in §4.8.8.

**Findings on the unseen test split.**
- **Tolerant mAP50 rises 21% over v2.7 on average** (0.0298 → 0.0360). v2.8 is best on 6 of 8 conditions: clean +26%,
  low light +80%, low-res +33%, rain-haze +30%.
- **Strict mAP50 is a tie** (0.0196 against 0.0192).
- **Small regressions on JPEG and glare (strict).** The validation gain from edge denoise on JPEG did not carry over at
  span level, and glare is slightly worse than v2.7 because CLAHE's darkness trigger is off.
- **Motion blur is the open problem.** Enhancement *off* beats both v2.7 and v2.8 (tolerant 0.0272), because unsharp
  masking sharpens the motion streak. A directional-blur detector that skips sharpening under motion is the clearest
  next step; it was not tuned here on test evidence.
- **Latency:** v2.8 is faster than v2.7 on degraded photos (4.3–5.5 s against 7–12 s including evaluation), because
  stitched spans rarely trigger the blur fallback.

##### 4.8.7 Abandoned wire: the topological detector

Swept over 108 settings on validation (`v28_loose_end_sweep_val.json`). A candidate counts as a hit
when it lies within 15 px of a labelled abandoned instance.

| Setting | Instances hit | Candidates | Precision (lower bound) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Best F | 1 / 6 | 38 | 0.026 |
| Best recall | 3 / 6 | 180 | 0.017 |

With the final v2.8 span constants, the detector still raises 213 candidates on the 22 validation
photos, about 10 per photo. A visual audit of 24 candidates (`figures/loose_end_candidates_val.jpg`)
found mostly two kinds:
- **Mid-wire mask gaps** that stitching did not bridge. The wire visibly continues through the
  candidate.
- **Real terminations at insulators, crossarms and poles.**

The few dangling shapes were jumper loops at insulators: normal installations, not abandoned wire.

Pseudo-label mining needs candidates with precision > 0.75, so it was **not attempted**. Self-training
on these candidates would teach WireNet its own mask gaps. The detector is shipped **disabled**
(`hazard.loose_ends: false`) as a documented research module.

This confirms §4.3.6 from a second direction. An abandoned wire differs from a live one by context
— a free end that is *not* at hardware, no termination — and recognising that context needs either
pole and hardware classes or many more labelled abandoned instances.

##### 4.8.8 End-to-end server verification

The real FastAPI app was run with the shipped configuration (YOLO + WireNet span mode, flip TTA, stitching, chord
gate, hazard and quality on) on a throw-away data directory. Uploads went through the public field-screener endpoint
(`docs/thesis/results/server_check_v28.json`): 3 test photos × {clean, glare, lowlight, motion}, plus 4 photos that
v2.8 rated low-risk.

| Photo | Condition | Status | Upload → result (s) | Verdict | Grade | Spans / scored | PDF |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 026-11-03 1455 | clean | complete | 2.83 | AT RISK - MODERATE | Retake recommended | 2 / 2 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1455 | glare | complete | 1.79 | AT RISK - HIGH | Retake recommended | 7 / 4 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1455 | lowlight | no_wires_detected | 1.20 | INCONCLUSIVE - RETAKE PHOTO | Retake recommended | 0 / 0 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1455 | motion | no_wires_detected | 3.26 | INCONCLUSIVE - RETAKE PHOTO | Retake recommended | 0 / 0 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1455 | clean | complete | 2.58 | AT RISK - HIGH | B | 35 / 21 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1455 | glare | complete | 2.88 | AT RISK - HIGH | B | 27 / 18 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1455 | lowlight | complete | 2.97 | AT RISK - HIGH | B | 34 / 25 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1455 | motion | complete | 2.24 | AT RISK - HIGH | B | 15 / 11 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1456 | clean | complete | 2.90 | AT RISK - HIGH | A | 39 / 26 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1456 | glare | complete | 2.66 | AT RISK - HIGH | B | 31 / 19 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1456 | lowlight | complete | 2.85 | AT RISK - HIGH | B | 35 / 27 | yes |
| 026-11-03 1456 | motion | complete | 2.27 | AT RISK - HIGH | B | 22 / 20 | yes |
| 026-11-03 2214 | clean | complete | 3.45 | NOT AT RISK | B | 37 / 19 | yes |
| 026-11-03 2137 | clean | complete | 3.19 | AT RISK - HIGH | B | 34 / 26 | yes |
| jpg.rf.7qcdqnj | clean | complete | 1.74 | NOT AT RISK | A | 5 / 5 | yes |
| jpg.rf.ZfhiIln | clean | complete | 1.48 | NOT AT RISK | A | 5 / 4 | yes |

Latency from upload to result was 1.20–3.45 s (median 2.83 s) on the M1 with MPS,
under the 4.5 s target. All 16 PDFs were valid and include the hazard section and the diagnostic figure.

**A failure mode found here and fixed.** In the first run, a low-light and a motion-blurred photo with no detectable
wires were reported as NOT AT RISK — a silent failure. The verdict now becomes **INCONCLUSIVE – RETAKE PHOTO** whenever
the reliability grade is *Retake recommended* and no hazard was found (`hazard.apply_reliability`). A hazard that was
found is kept, with a low-reliability note. Both runs are in the record; the table shows the run after the fix.

##### 4.8.9 Failure-mode audit (validation, v2.8, 22 images)

Spearman correlation of per-image tolerant pixel IoU with each image property. None is significant
at α = 0.05.

| Property | ρ | p |
|---|---|---|
| Labelled span count | 0.041 | 0.86 |
| Spans per megapixel of photo content | 0.040 | 0.86 |
| Median labelled wire width | −0.328 | 0.14 |
| Contrast (std of L\*) | −0.286 | 0.20 |
| Sharpness (variance of the Laplacian) | −0.309 | 0.16 |
| Mean L\* (brightness) | 0.405 | 0.06 |

Per image, **false positives outweigh misses**: 32–62% of predicted wire pixels have no label, while
10–34% of labelled wire pixels are missed. The hardest image (`figures/failure_hard_*.jpg`; tolerant
IoU 0.42, 35 labelled spans) shows where these come from:
- **False positives (red):** mostly in a dense bundle where many visible wires were never labelled,
  so these are *label incompleteness*, not model errors. A minority are true false positives on
  insulators and crossarm edges.
- **Misses (blue):** labelled wires crossing in front of the pole, against pole texture.

Accuracy in clean conditions is therefore limited about as much by label completeness in dense
bundles as by the model. Wire density alone does not predict accuracy (ρ = 0.04).

---

#### 4.9 Engineering additions

- **Diagnostic figure** (`app/services/diagnostic.py`): three panels — the photo; segmentation by
  class; and spans by CRS level with clearance lines, loose-end markers and the verdict and grade
  badges. It is embedded in every PDF report together with the hazard reasons.
- **Batch street audit** (`scripts/batch_inspect.py`): takes a folder or a survey video (frames every
  N s) and removes near-duplicate frames by pHash. It writes `inspection.csv`, `summary.json` /
  `summary.md`, the diagnostic figures, `rejected.csv` (corrupt, too small, constant images), and
  `review_queue/` — LabelMe 5 JSON with the model's polygons for high-risk and low-reliability
  photos, ready to be corrected into new training labels.
- **Robustness:** the API and the batch tool handle corrupt, tiny, constant, duplicate and non-image
  inputs, extreme aspect ratios (3000 × 320, 400 × 1600), photos with no wires, and vegetation-only
  photos. Each case is tested.
- **Tests:** 294 (v2.7) → 351, all passing; `ruff` clean.

#### 4.10 Limitations and next steps

1. **The risk verdict is not calibrated.** The δ_lim placeholder and the per-photo worst-of-30 rule
   (§4.8.2) need the engineer's reference ratings (H3) and a team decision on aggregation. The
   evidence above is offered for that decision.
2. **CRS treats every curve as span sag.** Service drops and slack leads need either a class of their
   own or a pole-to-pole span criterion.
3. **Glare is unsolved.** It is detected — the grade asks for a retake — but not corrected.
4. **Abandoned wire** still needs labels (§4.3.6), or pole and hardware classes for the topological
   approach.
5. **Small validation set.** With 22 images, differences of about 0.002 in tolerant mAP50 are noise.
   All constants should be re-validated when the labelled set grows.


---

## B.3 Report v2.9 (`docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.9_refinements.md`)

### Chapter 4 (continued) — v2.9 Refinements: Pole-Level Verdicts and Three Negative Results

Branch `v2.9-refinement`, from `dc1177b` (`v2.8-overnight-sprint`). No model was retrained. The WireNet
weights (`models/wirenet-r34.pt`, SHA-256 prefix `ab2353c7`) and the YOLO detector are byte-identical to v2.7
and v2.8. Every rule and constant below was decided on the 22 validation photos. The 21 test photos were
used only for the final tables (§4.11.6–4.11.8). §4.11.6 records why the test split was evaluated more
than once, with every superseded run kept.

This chapter follows the protocol of the v2.8 chapter (§4.7): cached model outputs where possible, coordinate
changes scored on validation, differences below about 0.002 tolerant mAP50 treated as ties, and the
anti-regression guard (validation clean tolerant mAP50 ≥ 0.0397, power ≥ 0.525 and telecom ≥ 0.418 tolerant
pixel IoU, 8-condition mean not lower than v2.8's 0.3872).

---

#### 4.11 v2.9 at a glance

| Upgrade | Decision | Evidence (validation) |
|---|---|---|
| 1a. Motion-blur detector (structure-tensor anisotropy) | **Kept as a measurement** (it names "camera shake" in the reliability reason) | Separates 22/22 motion photos from 22/22 defocus and 3/3 soft clean photos at one threshold |
| 1b. Route motion-blurred photos away from unsharp masking | **Rejected** | Pixel IoU +0.021 power, but span tolerant mAP50 0.0177 → 0.0098–0.0143 in all four variants |
| 1c. JPEG deblocking (8 px boundary filter) | **Rejected** | Tolerant mAP50 tie (0.0319 → 0.0316); strict 0.0058 → 0.0043 |
| 2a. Service drop / guy wire split (chord > 35°) | **Rejected after a visual audit** (code kept, off) | Steep wires are *not* the ones that read high, and 24 of 24 audited "drops" were spans seen steeply in upward-looking photos (§4.11.2) |
| 2b. Corroborated pole-level aggregation (binomial test on test–retest p0) | **Adopted** | Verdicts high / moderate / not: 17 / 3 / 2 → 2 / 10 / 10 (test: 18 / 3 / 0 → 7 / 7 / 7) |
| 2c. Low-hanging service drop (framing proxy) | **Off** (depends on 2a) | Fired on 4 of 22 photos, on misclassified spans |
| 3. X-junction tangent-continuity matching | **Rejected** | 0.0353 (far tangents) or exactly 0.0397 (near tangents) against 0.0397 |
| 4. Diagnostic figure, grade reasons, web badge, defense summary | **Adopted** | §4.11.5, tests |

**Three of the proposed techniques produced negative results** (motion routing, the drop split,
X-junction matching). They are reported in full, because each answers a question a panel will ask: "why not
turn off sharpening for motion blur?", "why not separate drop wires?", "why not untangle crossings?".

---

#### 4.11.1 Mission 1 — Motion blur, defocus and JPEG blocking

##### Method: a content-normalised, direction-coherent blur measure (`app/pipeline/enhance.py`)

The variance of the Laplacian (v2.8's blur measure) falls under both motion blur and defocus. Only motion
blur removes detail **along one direction**. A plain gradient structure tensor cannot see this in street
photos, because the content is itself anisotropic: parallel wires and facades produce strongly oriented
gradients in a sharp photo (raw eigenvalue anisotropy: median 0.48 clean, 0.72 motion — overlapping).

The measure therefore normalises out the content:

1. Per 128 px tile, two structure tensors:
   - J_f, from the photo's gradients (Sobel);
   - J_c, from the gradients of a σ = 2 px Gaussian copy.
2. The whitened tensor M = J_c^{−1/2} J_f J_c^{−1/2}.
   - Content orientation appears in both tensors and cancels.
   - A blur kernel attenuates fine detail more than coarse detail. A *linear* kernel does so only along its
     direction, so M becomes anisotropic.
3. Tile anisotropy a = 1 − λ_min/λ_max, kept with its direction as a doubled-angle vector a·e^{2iθ}. Tiles in
   padding or with almost no coarse gradient energy (sky) are skipped.
4. **Motion coherence** = |mean vector|: high only when the lost direction agrees across the photo. A camera
   shake has one direction; content anisotropy does not.

A photo is classed as motion-blurred when it is soft (Laplacian variance ≤ 60, the existing unsharp trigger),
its coherence is ≥ 0.16, and it is **not overexposed** (clipped share < 0.25, the existing glare trigger).

**Why the overexposure guard.** In the first calibration, 3 of 16 glare photos were routed as motion. All had
58–83% clipped pixels: their low Laplacian variance comes from lost contrast, not blur, and their anisotropy
is the content's. The guard removed all three (0/16).

##### Calibration (`v29_motion_calib_val.json`, figure `v29_motion_router_val.png`)

Photos routed as motion, out of photos soft enough to be sharpened, per stress condition:

| Coherence threshold | clean | glare | **motion** | defocus | jpeg | lowres | rainhaze |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.10 | 1/3 | 0/16 | 22/22 | 3/22 | 1/2 | 9/17 | 6/9 |
| 0.14 | 1/3 | 0/16 | 22/22 | 0/22 | 1/2 | 7/17 | 1/9 |
| **0.16** | **0/3** | **0/16** | **22/22** | **0/22** | 1/2 | 4/17 | 1/9 |
| 0.20 | 0/3 | 0/16 | 18/22 | 0/22 | 0/2 | 3/17 | 1/9 |

0.16 is the smallest threshold that keeps every clean and defocused photo out of the motion route while
catching every motion photo. The low-resolution photos it catches are resampling-blurred. At pixel level they
score the same with or without sharpening (0.517 / 0.408 against 0.517 / 0.409), so misrouting them costs
nothing.

**Caveat.** The motion condition is synthetic: a linear kernel, 1.5% of the long side, at a random angle. Real
hand shake is curved, so coherence on real photos will be lower. The threshold has not been checked against
real motion-blurred field photos.

##### Routing variants (`v29_stress_pixel_val*.jsonl`, `v29_stress_spans_val*.jsonl`)

Pixel level (tolerant IoU, power / telecom):

| Variant | motion | jpeg | glare | 8-condition mean |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| v2.8 | 0.357 / 0.277 | 0.490 / 0.400 | 0.079 / 0.185 | 0.3872 |
| + motion route, bilateral smoothing | 0.378 / 0.270 | 0.493 / 0.402 | 0.079 / 0.185 | 0.3886 |
| + motion route, skip unsharp only | **0.381 / 0.276** | 0.493 / 0.402 | 0.079 / 0.185 | **0.3892** |
| + deblocking | 0.357 / 0.277 | 0.491 / 0.401 | 0.079 / 0.185 | 0.3873 |
| + deblocking replacing edge denoise | 0.357 / 0.277 | 0.487 / 0.397 | 0.079 / 0.185 | 0.3868 |
| + motion route + deblocking | 0.378 / 0.270 | 0.494 / 0.403 | 0.079 / 0.185 | 0.3888 |

Clean, low-light and defocus are identical for every variant (0.528 / 0.422, 0.512 / 0.368, 0.403 / 0.314).

At pixel level, skipping the unsharp mask under motion blur is the best variant, as v2.8 predicted. **It does
not survive at span level**, which is what the screener uses.

Span level, the full screener pipeline (validation, motion condition; final code, `v29_stress_spans_val.jsonl`):

| Variant | Strict mAP50 | Tolerant mAP50 | Power tol. AP50 / R | Telecom tol. AP50 / R | s/img* |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **v2.8 = v2.9** | 0.0051 | **0.0177** | 0.032 / 0.111 | 0.018 / 0.048 | 6.1 |
| route, smoothing | 0.0039 | 0.0143 | 0.036 / 0.111 | 0.005 / 0.012 | 4.7 |
| route, skip unsharp | **0.0064** | 0.0124 | 0.021 / 0.105 | 0.003 / 0.024 | 5.5 |
| route, smoothing; fallback sharpens | 0.0038 | 0.0143 | 0.037 / 0.111 | 0.005 / 0.012 | 5.0 |
| route, skip; fallback sharpens | 0.0039 | 0.0098 | 0.021 / 0.105 | 0.003 / 0.024 | 5.9 |

\* Including evaluation.

**What happened.**
1. **The first span-level run exposed a coupling** (`superseded/v29_stress_spans_val_run1*.jsonl`). v2.8's
   blur fallback (a second, stronger pass with a mirrored merge, `runner.finalize_detections`) is triggered
   by the *same* decision that requests unsharp masking. Routing a photo away from unsharp silently switched
   its fallback off: tolerant 0.0148 / 0.0093 at 3.5–3.8 s per photo. The coupling was fixed (motion photos
   stay eligible for the fallback, `runner._degraded`), and the table above is the re-run.
2. **With the fallback restored, every routed variant is still below v2.8 in tolerant mAP50**, by
   0.0034–0.0079, above the 0.002 noise band. Skipping unsharp gains strict mAP50 (+0.0013, inside the
   noise band). The loss is concentrated in telecom: AP50 falls from 0.018 to 0.003–0.005 and recall from
   0.048 to 0.012–0.024. A plausible mechanism, not verified here: sharpening across a motion streak keeps
   the thin telecom cables connected enough to skeletonise into spans. Without sharpening they fragment
   below the minimum span length.
3. **Pixel IoU and span AP disagree.** Pixel IoU rewards covering wire pixels; span AP needs a continuous,
   correctly separated instance. This is the second time the gap has decided a design (the first was
   YOLO-seg's mask ceiling, v2.7 §4.3.1).

**Decision.** The router is **not adopted** (`enhancement.motion_route: false`). The anti-regression rule
requires the degraded-condition score not to fall, and it falls. The detector stays in use for one purpose: a
low-reliability photo that is soft and direction-coherent is explained to the field worker as "Camera shake
(motion blur) smeared the conductors" rather than "out of focus" (§4.11.5).

**JPEG deblocking**, an H.264-style boundary filter, works as a filter: blockiness falls 3.17 → 1.57 at q20,
and real edges above the step threshold are untouched (tested). It is not adopted. At span level it ties in
tolerant mAP50 (0.0319 → 0.0316) and loses in strict mAP50 (0.0058 → 0.0043). Power recall rises
(0.172 → 0.201), but the masks are less precise. It runs at the original resolution before any resize,
because the 8 px grid is aligned only there. This matters for real 12 MP uploads, which v2.8's working-scale
blockiness measure could not see.

---

#### 4.11.2 Mission 2 — Service drops and the per-photo aggregation rule

##### 2a. Are steep wires the ones that read high? (`v29_span_geometry_summary_val.json`, figure `v29_inclination_val.png`)

The directive's premise was that steep service drops, scored with span sag equations, cause the "always high"
reading. The premise was **checked before it was acted on**. 657 scored spans on validation (single pass, no
TTA), by chord inclination from the image horizontal:

| Inclination | Spans | High band | Medium band | Median chord (px) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0–10° | 196 | 12.8% | 13.3% | 242 |
| 10–20° | 147 | 14.3% | 7.5% | 217 |
| 20–35° | 145 | 6.9% | 10.3% | 241 |
| 35–50° | 91 | 2.2% | 5.5% | 335 |
| 50–90° | 78 | 1.3% | 5.1% | 321 |

**The premise is false on this data.** Steep wires (26% of scored spans) are 5–10 times *less* likely to read
high than flat ones.

The geometry explains why. Sag is measured perpendicular to the chord. For a span of horizontal length S
inclined at θ, with vertical mid-span sag D, the chord is L = S / cos θ and the perpendicular sag is about
D cos θ. So δ/L ≈ (D/S) cos²θ, which shrinks as θ grows.

**The split was implemented anyway, for physical correctness.**
- Chapter III's allowable sag ratio (Eq. 9) comes from the tension–span relation of a pole-to-pole
  distribution span. A service drop to a building, or a guy wire, is not such a span.
- A catenary y(x) is ill-conditioned for near-vertical wires.

Chords steeper than 35° (the directive's value) were given the status `service_drop`. Fragments below the
100 px precision gate were kept `too_short`.

**Then it was audited, and rejected** (`figures/v29_drop_audit_val.jpg`, `ml/experiments/drop_audit.py`).
- The diagnostic figure of a validation photo showed 18 cyan "drops". Most were long power-line runs that
  converge steeply towards the vanishing point because the photo looks up at the pole.
- A random sample of 24 of the 200 validation "drops" (seed 0) was then inspected. Essentially all are
  straight conductors running inside parallel bundles: pole-to-pole spans seen in perspective. At most two
  are ambiguous (a mask on a pole, a bundle entering a crossarm), and none is clearly a drop to a building.
- **Image-space inclination is a property of the camera, not of the wire.** Separating drops from spans
  needs 3-D cues: where the wire ends (a building wall rather than a crossarm), the vanishing-point
  geometry, or a labelled `service-drop` class.

**Decision.** The split is **off** (`crs.max_span_inclination_deg: 0`); the code stays as a configurable
option, with tests. Two coupling bugs it exposed were fixed and kept, because they matter for any future
status that is "measured but not scored":
1. Fragments must meet the precision gate before being classified.
2. The blur fallback must not treat a classified wire as a failed one (`runner._measured`).

Rejecting the split also restores the steep spans' CRS scoring. That is consistent with the geometric
argument above: δ/L shrinks with cos²θ, so steep spans are *under*-scored if anything, never over-scored.

##### 2b. Corroborated aggregation

**Why the v2.8 rule saturates.** v2.8 sets a photo's verdict from its single worst span. A pole carries a
median of 29.5 measurable spans (validation). Even a small per-span false-alarm rate p therefore saturates
the photo verdict: 1 − (1 − p)^n. At p = 0.05 and n = 29.5 that is 0.78.

**Test–retest: measuring p0 instead of assuming it** (`ml/experiments/span_geometry.py`,
`v29_span_geometry_summary_all_val.json`).
- Each validation photo is measured twice: as is, and mirrored (with TTA off, so the two passes differ).
- Spans of the same class are matched by tolerant mask IoU ≥ 0.3: 492 matched pairs of scored spans.
- A span "high" in one measurement and not in the other is a level the measurement cannot reproduce. p0 is
  the per-measurement discordance.

| Quantity (all scored spans) | Value |
|---|---|
| p0 (high) | **0.050** |
| p0 (medium or above) | **0.090** |
| CRS = 10 (saturated) spans whose retest was also high | 13 of 35 (37%) |

The last row rules out the obvious shortcut, a "single extreme span" override: even saturated scores
reproduce in barely a third of cases.

*Provenance note.* p0 was first measured on spans up to 35° only (0.058 / 0.100, 356 pairs,
`v29_span_geometry_summary_val.json`), while the drop split was on. When the split was turned off
(§4.11.2a), the rule came to apply to all spans, so p0 was re-measured on the same retest data over all
spans. Only validation data was used. The verdict runs made with the earlier values are kept in
`results/superseded/`.

**The rule** (`hazard.assess`, `sag_rule: corroborated`). For a photo with n measurable spans:
- k*(n) = min { k : P(Bin(n, p0) ≥ k) ≤ α }, with α = 0.05 (the conventional level, not tuned).
- **HIGH** needs at least k*(n) spans in the high band, using p0 = 0.050.
- **MODERATE** needs at least k*(n) spans at medium or above, using p0 = 0.090.
- Spans in a band that do not reach k* are **listed as a NOTE**, with their numbers, the count found and
  the count needed, and "verify visually". They are never dropped silently.
- The vegetation rules (power contact → HIGH, ≤ 15 px → MODERATE, telecom contact → MODERATE) are
  **unchanged and absolute**.

| Measurable spans n | 1 | 2 | 5 | 10 | 22 | 30 (≈ median) | 49 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| k* for HIGH (p0 = 0.050) | 1 | 2 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 |
| k* for MODERATE (p0 = 0.090) | 2 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 9 |

**Two edge cases a panel should hear plainly.**
- With exactly one measurable span, one high span is significant (P = p0 = 0.050 ≤ α). The boundary is
  that thin. One medium span is not (0.090 > α), and is reported as a NOTE.
- The trade-off between missed and false alarms is one explicit policy parameter, `hazard.alpha`. A team
  that wants more sensitivity lowers k* by raising α.

**What this rule is and is not.**
- It is a significance test: "more spans read high than measurement noise explains".
- It is *not* a claim about the true hazard rate, which needs the engineer's reference ratings (H3).
- p0 comes from single-pass measurements. The deployed pipeline averages a flip pass, which is less noisy,
  so p0 is if anything overestimated. That errs towards fewer alarms, and the NOTE lines keep every
  uncorroborated span visible to the reviewer.
- Spans of one photo share lighting and blur, so their errors are not independent. Correlated false
  highs make clusters more likely than the binomial predicts, so the rule is somewhat permissive in bad
  photos. The reliability grade is the safeguard there.
- The rule says nothing about whether the placeholder δ_lim is right; v2.8 §4.8.2 still applies.

##### 2c. Low-hanging service drops

A proxy raising MODERATE when a service drop's lowest point lay in the bottom 15% of the photo content was
implemented and tested. It is **off**, because its input (the drop classification) failed the audit above.
It fired on 4 of 22 validation photos, all on misclassified spans.

##### 2d. Verdicts (validation: `v29_verdicts_val.json` for the rule ablation, `v29_verdicts_pipeline_val.json` for the full pipeline)

Rule ablation on identical spans and vegetation:

| Rule | High | Moderate | Not at risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| v2.8 (any span) | 17 | 3 | 2 |
| + service-drop split (rejected) | 17 | 2 | 3 |
| **corroborated only = v2.9** | **2** | **10** | **10** |
| split + corroborated (rejected) | 3 | 8 | 11 |
| split + corroborated + low-drop proxy (rejected) | 3 | 10 | 9 |

(p0 = 0.050 / 0.090 for every corroborated row, `v29_verdicts_val.json`.)

**The corroboration rule does the work.** The split changes one photo on its own. Combined with
corroboration it moves one photo the *wrong* way (to HIGH), because removing steep spans lowers n and so k*.
This is consistent with §2a.

Drivers of the v2.9 verdicts (validation, full shipped pipeline, `v29_verdicts_pipeline_val.json`):

| Driver | Photos |
|---|---|
| Corroborated high sag | 2 |
| Corroborated medium-or-above sag | 5 |
| Telecom in contact with vegetation (sag not corroborated) | 5 |
| Not at risk | 10, of which 8 carry a NOTE listing uncorroborated spans |

The full pipeline gives the same distribution as the ablation (2 / 10 / 10). No validation photo is
INCONCLUSIVE: no photo was graded Retake.

---

#### 4.11.3 Mission 3 — X-junction tangent continuity (`v29_xjunction_val.jsonl`)

At a junction with four (or six) piece ends, the pieces are paired by the **best perfect matching** of
tangent continuity. The matching is accepted only if:
- every pair is anti-parallel within the tolerance τ;
- the paired wires cross at more than 30°.

Otherwise v2.8's greedy rule applies. Tangents were first measured 4–20 px from the end, because the
skeleton bends inside a junction.

| Variant (all else v2.8) | Strict mAP50 | Tolerant mAP50 | Power tol. AP50 | Telecom tol. AP50 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **v2.8** | 0.0068 | **0.0397** | 0.108 | 0.022 |
| matching, far tangents, τ = 15° / 20° / 25° | 0.0063 | 0.0353 | 0.093 | 0.019 |
| matching, v2.8 near tangents, τ = 15° / 20° / 25° | 0.0068 | 0.0397 | 0.108 | 0.022 |

**Reading.**
- With v2.8's tangents, the optimal matching makes exactly the pairings the greedy rule already makes: at a
  clean X crossing, "straightest pair first" *is* the optimal matching.
- The far-tangent estimator is worse (−0.0044). At 4–20 px from a junction, the piece often already
  belongs to a neighbouring bundle wire.
- Neither variant beats 0.0397, so the option ships **off** (`xjunction_tol_deg: 0`).

The remaining fragmentation is not at crossings. It is at mask dropouts and in near-parallel bundles,
where no geometric pairing rule can tell the wires apart at 2–3 px width.

---

#### 4.11.4 Anti-regression guard

v2.9 ships no change to the segmentation path: motion routing, deblocking, X-junction matching and the drop
split are all off. The full-pipeline span evaluation on validation is identical, digit for digit, to v2.8:

| Validation | v2.8 strict / tolerant | v2.9 strict / tolerant |
|---|---|---|
| clean | 0.0068 / 0.0394 | 0.0068 / 0.0394 |
| motion | 0.0051 / 0.0177 | 0.0051 / 0.0177 |
| jpeg | 0.0058 / 0.0319 | 0.0058 / 0.0319 |
| glare | 0.0064 / 0.0075 | 0.0064 / 0.0075 |

On the cached-span harness of v2.8 §4.8.1, tolerant mAP50 is 0.0397, the same span constants as before. The
full-pipeline evaluator gives 0.0394 because it includes the blur fallback. Power / telecom tolerant pixel
IoU is 0.528 / 0.422, and the 8-condition pixel mean is 0.3872. **Guard: pass.** The only new per-photo
computation is the coherence measurement, about 0.1 s.

---

#### 4.11.5 Mission 4 — Demonstration and report output

**Diagnostic figure** (`app/services/diagnostic.py`; PDF and batch tool). Example:
`figures/v29_diagnostic_example.jpg`.

1. **Original + reliability badge:** "Grade A/B/C (R = 0.xx)" or "RETAKE RECOMMENDED" / "INCONCLUSIVE –
   RETAKE PHOTO", with the reason in plain English.
2. **Segmentation** with a class legend: power blue, telecom violet, abandoned orange, vegetation green.
3. **Spans vs drops:**
   - primary spans filled by CRS level and labelled S*n*;
   - service drops in cyan, labelled D*n* (shown only if the drop split is enabled; off in v2.9);
   - a level legend with the drop count;
   - vegetation clearance lines labelled "*px* ~*m* ± *u* m". The metre value is the midpoint of the
     ±1 px wire-width range; the uncertainty is half its width.
4. **A strip of verdict reasons** under the panels.

**Reliability reasons** (`quality.cause`). Every grade below A names the most likely cause, checked in the
order a field worker can act on:
1. Severe sky glare washed out the overhead conductors
2. Too dark
3. Almost no wire pixels
4. Camera shake (motion blur, from the §4.11.1 detector)
5. Out of focus
6. Bright-sky glare lowering contrast
7. Low light
8. Model uncertainty (clutter)

The wording never changes the grade.

**Web field screener.**
- A coloured grade badge, with the reason.
- Service drops, if enabled, are listed as "service drop", not "not measured".

**PDF.**
- Reason line under the grade.
- A per-wire note for drops.
- A caption that states that metre values are estimates, not survey measurements.

---

#### 4.11.6 Test split: verdicts (21 photos, `v29_verdicts_pipeline_test.json`)

| | High | Moderate | Not at risk | Inconclusive |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| v2.8 | 18 | 3 | 0 | 0 |
| **v2.9** | **7** | **7** | **7** | 0 |

Drivers of the v2.9 test verdicts:

| Driver | Photos |
|---|---|
| Corroborated high sag (one also with telecom–vegetation contact) | 7 |
| Corroborated medium-or-above sag | 3 |
| Telecom in contact with vegetation only | 4 |
| Not at risk | 7, each with a NOTE listing uncorroborated spans |

On test, the corroboration rule keeps 7 poles at HIGH: more of their spans read high than noise explains.
On validation only 2 did. The rule removes the saturation without removing the high verdict. Whether these 7
poles are the right ones cannot be judged without reference ratings (H3).

Figure: `figures/v29_verdicts.png`.

**How many times the test split was evaluated, and why.** No threshold or rule was chosen from test. All
superseded runs are kept in `docs/thesis/results/superseded/`.
1. **First locked run.** It had the drop split on, and its tolerant 8-condition mean was 0.0355 against
   v2.8's 0.0360 (`superseded/v29_stress_spans_test_run1.jsonl`). Its verdicts were 7 / 9 / 5
   (`superseded/v29_verdicts_pipeline_test_run1.json`). The segmentation path should not have changed at
   all, so the difference was traced. It came from a coupling bug: the blur fallback counted service drops
   as failed wires. That was fixed, which is a correctness fix, not a tuning step.
2. **Validation then exposed the drop misclassification** (§4.11.2a), and the split was turned off.
3. **The final run** uses the configuration in this commit. Its segmentation results equal v2.8's on every
   condition (§4.11.7).
4. **The verdict layer only (not segmentation) was re-run once more on test**, after p0 was re-measured on
   validation over all scored spans (§4.11.2b provenance note). With the earlier subset value (0.058 / 0.100)
   the test verdicts were 6 / 7 / 8 (`superseded/v29_verdicts_pipeline_test_run2_p0subset.json`). With the
   final value they are 7 / 7 / 7.

The real FastAPI app with the shipped configuration and installed models, on a throw-away data directory
(`scripts/server_check.py`, now a repeatable script). 5 test photos: 3 under clean, low-light, motion and
glare conditions, and 2 clean.

| Photo | Condition | Status | Upload → result (s) | Verdict | Grade | Grade reason | Spans / scored |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 145516 | clean | complete | 2.67 | AT RISK – MODERATE | Retake | model uncertain (clutter / low contrast) | 2 / 2 |
| 145516 | lowlight | no wires | 1.34 | INCONCLUSIVE – RETAKE | Retake | almost no wire pixels | 0 / 0 |
| 145516 | motion | no wires | 3.39 | INCONCLUSIVE – RETAKE | Retake | almost no wire pixels | 0 / 0 |
| 145516 | glare | complete | 1.63 | AT RISK – MODERATE | Retake | bright-sky glare lowered contrast | 7 / 4 |
| 145557 | clean | complete | 2.45 | AT RISK – HIGH | B | model uncertain | 35 / 21 |
| 145557 | lowlight | complete | 2.97 | AT RISK – HIGH | B | low light | 34 / 25 |
| 145557 | motion | complete | 2.14 | AT RISK – HIGH | B | camera shake (motion blur) | 15 / 11 |
| 145557 | glare | complete | 2.55 | NOT AT RISK | B | bright-sky glare lowered contrast | 27 / 18 |
| 145603 | clean | complete | 2.80 | NOT AT RISK | A | – | 39 / 26 |
| 145603 | lowlight | complete | 2.82 | AT RISK – MODERATE | B | low light | 35 / 27 |
| 145603 | motion | complete | 2.35 | AT RISK – HIGH | B | camera shake (motion blur) | 22 / 20 |
| 145603 | glare | complete | 2.49 | NOT AT RISK | B | bright-sky glare lowered contrast | 31 / 19 |
| 145634 | clean | complete | 4.58 | AT RISK – MODERATE | B | model uncertain | 81 / 57 |
| 213707 | clean | complete | 3.17 | NOT AT RISK | B | model uncertain | 37 / 34 |

**Latency** was 1.34–4.58 s, median 2.67 s, on the Apple-silicon Mac with MPS. Thirteen of 14 uploads
finished under the 4.5 s target. The 81-span pole took 4.58 s (4.50 s on an earlier run of the same check):
per-span fitting dominates on very dense poles, so **the 4.5 s target is not met for the densest scenes**.
Motion coherence is now computed only for soft photos, the only ones it is used for.

**PDFs:** all 14 were valid and include the reliability reason and the v2 diagnostic figure.

**Read the verdicts with the grades.**
- Under glare, photo 145557 reads AT RISK – HIGH on the clean upload but NOT AT RISK at grade B. Glare
  removes spans (power recall 0.04 on test), and the B grade does not fully capture that loss.
- This is a limit of the reliability score (ρ = 0.64), not of the verdict rule. A low-contrast photo can
  look confident to the model.
- On photo 145516 the glare result is graded Retake. A hazard was still found (corroborated medium sag), so it
  stays AT RISK – MODERATE with a low-reliability note, as designed.

---

#### 4.11.9 Limitations

- **δ_lim is still the placeholder 0.040.** The corroboration rule decides how many spans must agree. It
  does not decide whether "high" is high. Calibration needs the engineer's reference ratings (H3).
- **p0 comes from a single type of retest (mirroring).** A capture–recapture of the same pole from a few
  metres away would be a stronger test–retest and would include viewpoint noise.
- **Drop wires are still scored as spans.** Telling them apart needs 3-D cues or a labelled class
  (§4.11.2a). Image-space inclination failed the audit.
- **The motion detector is calibrated on synthetic linear blur.** The routing result is a negative result
  for *this* WireNet; a model trained with motion-blur augmentation might respond differently.
- **22 validation photos.** Every decision above is a decision on 22 photos, with the noise band stated
  where it applies.

#### 4.11.10 Reproduction

python -m ml.experiments.stress_bench --split val --out docs/thesis/results/v29_stress_pixel_val_r2.jsonl --variants v2.8+motion v2.8+motion_skip v2.8+motion+deblock
python -m scripts.stress_test_all_conditions --split val --out docs/thesis/results/v29_stress_spans_val.jsonl --configs v2.8 v2.9 --conditions clean motion jpeg glare
python -m scripts.stress_test_all_conditions --split val --out docs/thesis/results/v29_stress_spans_val.jsonl --conditions motion --configs v2.8+motion v2.8+motion_skip v2.8+motion_fbu v2.8+motion_skip_fbu
python -m scripts.stress_test_all_conditions --split val --out docs/thesis/results/v29_stress_spans_val.jsonl --conditions jpeg --configs v2.8+deblock
