## B.6 Report v2.12 (`docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.12_ensemble.md`)

### Chapter 4 (continued) — v2.12: Complementary Backbones, Probability Fusion and Monocular Depth

Branch `v2.12-multi-model-ensemble`, from `eff526a` (v2.11). The shipped WireNet-R34 (`models/wirenet-r34.pt`,
`ab2353c7`) and every earlier commit are untouched; new checkpoints are saved separately as `models/v212_*.pt`.
Every decision was made on the 22 validation photos. The 21 test photos were used once, after v2.12 was frozen
(§4.14.7).

**Hardware.** All training, inference, evaluation and latency runs in this report, and in every earlier version
from v2.6 onwards, were executed locally on one **Apple Silicon M1 laptop with 8 GB of unified memory**
(`hw.memsize` 8,589,934,592; PyTorch MPS backend for the GPU, CPU otherwise). CPU and GPU share those 8 GB, so
model choice, batch size and concurrency below are bounded by memory, not only by accuracy. No cloud or external
GPU was used.

| Mission | What was found | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| Complementary backbones | MiT-B1 (transformer) and ConvNeXt-Tiny (ConvNet) U-Nets trained with the WireNet recipe. Each alone beats R34 at span level on validation (tolerant mAP50 0.0429 and 0.0458 against 0.0397), but each loses telecom pixel F1. | Both kept as separate files (`models/v212_*.pt`); neither replaces R34 |
| Probability fusion | R34 0.25 + MiT-B1 0.5 + ConvNeXt 0.25: span tolerant mAP50 **0.0397 → 0.0537**, power / telecom tolerant F1 0.792 / 0.677 → 0.819 / 0.684. Beats R34 in 94.4% of paired bootstrap resamples. Passes the accuracy gate. | **Not shipped: 4.7 s per photo, 5.5 s on the dense pole**, over the 3.5 s limit on the 8 GB M1. Kept as an opt-in spec, `models/v212_ensemble.json` |
| Fusion without the mirror pass | Fits the time limit (about 2.6–2.7 s) but keeps less gain (0.045–0.050), and it changes the inputs of the reliability grade and the verdict's p0, both calibrated on R34. | Not adopted (re-calibration would be needed; selection after ~30 tries is optimistic) |
| Depth Anything V2 vegetation guard | The depth gate removes the night-sky false positive but also true trees (vegetation AP50 0.024 → 0.0025). Photometric gates are safe but miss it. | **Rejected**; limitation kept |
| Depth and sag | High-band share rises with depth recession, 3.2% → 10.4% (Fisher p = 0.0039). | **Finding**, no correction (no metric depth, no ground truth) |
| Shipped pipeline | v2.12 ships v2.11's pipeline. Whole-pipeline golden check 22/22 identical; R34 hash unchanged. | Test split run once (§4.14.7) |

---

#### 4.14.1 Why complementary backbones

WireNet-R34 is a convolutional U-Net. Its errors (v2.7 §4.3, v2.8 §4.8.9) are mostly broken thin spans and
power/telecom confusion where conductors run in parallel bundles. Two kinds of backbone could err differently:

- a **hierarchical transformer** (SegFormer's Mix Transformer, MiT), whose self-attention relates distant parts of a
  long thin structure; and
- a **modern ConvNet** (ConvNeXt), with larger kernels and a different inductive bias from ResNet.

If their errors are not shared, averaging class probabilities could recover spans that each model alone breaks.

#### 4.14.2 Backbone probe under the 8 GB memory limit

Before any training, each candidate was timed for one training step (batch 4 × 512², the R34 recipe) and one
1024² inference, on the M1 GPU (MPS):

| Backbone (decoder) | Parameters | Train step | 1024² inference | Peak GPU memory | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ResNet-34 (U-Net), shipped | 24.4 M | 0.92 s | 0.27 s | — | baseline |
| MiT-B1 (U-Net) | 16.4 M | 1.15 s | 0.46 s | 2.4 GB | **trained** |
| ConvNeXt-Tiny (U-Net) | — | 2.11 s | 0.60 s | 3.6 GB | **trained** |
| HRNet-W18 (U-Net) | — | 2.01 s | 0.55 s | 2.5 GB | not trained: same ConvNet family as ConvNeXt, slower; one ConvNet was budgeted |
| MiT-B2 (U-Net) | — | out of memory at batch 4 | — | — | rejected |
| SegFormer-B2 (native all-MLP head) | — | 2.63 s | **2.31 s** | — | rejected: inference alone would dominate the 3.5 s budget |

A probe that loaded several models into one process at the same time pushed the machine into swap and had to be
killed; the live inspection server was stopped for the rest of the sprint to free memory. Both trained models
therefore use **batch 2** (instead of 4); the training resolution, crops, loss and validation are otherwise the R34
recipe.

#### 4.14.3 Training (`ml/wirenet.py --encoder`, logs `models/v212_*.log.json`)

The trainer is the v2.7 WireNet trainer, now taking `--encoder` and `--arch`. Recipe, identical for both:

- data: the 102 training photos, 1024² letterbox, random 512² crops (80% centred on a wire);
- loss: cross-entropy + Dice + clDice; AdamW, one-cycle learning rate, peak 3·10⁻⁴;
- ImageNet-pretrained encoders (smp `mit_b1`; timm `tu-convnext_tiny`);
- **a full validation pass on all 22 photos after every epoch**; checkpoints kept for the best strict wire mIoU
  (`v212_*.pt`), the best tolerant wire IoU (`*-best-tolerant.pt`) and the last epoch (`*-last.pt`).

Each checkpoint writes an `.arch.json` sidecar (`{"encoder", "arch", "classes"}`). The application reads it, so
any of them can be loaded without code changes, and the shipped R34 file (no sidecar) loads as before.

| Model | Epochs | Batch | Wall time | Best epoch (strict) | Validation tolerant IoU power / telecom (training metric) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WireNet-R34 (v2.7, for reference) | 40 | 4 | — | — | 0.525 / 0.418 |
| MiT-B1 U-Net (`f595ef68`) | 40 | 2 | 4.66 h (286–779 s per epoch) | 32 | 0.543 / 0.404 |
| ConvNeXt-Tiny U-Net (`9633cee8`) | 20 | 2 | 3.39 h (515–773 s per epoch) | 16 | 0.540 / 0.415 |

The per-epoch times vary because the laptop throttles under sustained load. The best-tolerant checkpoints are
epoch 30 for MiT-B1 (0.546 / 0.413) and epoch 16 for ConvNeXt (the same checkpoint). The strict-best checkpoints
(`v212_*.pt`) are the ones evaluated below, as for R34. The weight files are not committed (66 MB and 128 MB); their
SHA-256 prefixes are given above and the logs and sidecars are committed.

**Data loading.** As in every WireNet run, the trainer decodes all training and validation photos once at start-up
and keeps the images and label masks in RAM as `uint8` arrays; only the random crops and augmentations are computed
per step, so there is no disk I/O during training.

ConvNeXt-Tiny got 20 epochs instead of 40 to finish within the session; 40 epochs would have taken about 7 hours
at the measured pace. Its full one-cycle schedule over those 20 epochs, end-to-end fine-tuning from epoch 1 and
validation on all 22 photos after every epoch were kept, so checkpoint selection is the same as for the other
models. Its validation curve had flattened (epochs 16–20: tolerant power 0.534–0.540, telecom 0.407–0.415).

**A schedule that was planned and not run.** A shorter 14-epoch schedule for ConvNeXt (encoder frozen for epochs
1–3, then cosine decay, validation from epoch 4) was briefly queued to save time. It was cancelled before it ran, in
favour of the standard recipe above, so the comparison between backbones is not confounded by different schedules.
The code for it was removed; no result in this report comes from it.

#### 4.14.4 Probability fusion (`ml/experiments/ensemble_eval.py`, `v212_ensemble_val.jsonl`)

Each model's flip-averaged class probabilities on the 22 validation photos are cached
(`ml.experiments.cache --wirenet`). The fused map is a weighted mean, p = Σ wᵢ pᵢ, with weights on a 0.25 simplex
grid. Every candidate is scored at two levels:

- **pixel**: 2 px tolerant IoU, precision, recall and F1 for power and telecom, plus the power↔telecom confusion
  rate (the share of a class's labelled pixels predicted as the other class);
- **span**: strict and tolerant span mAP50 from the shipped span assembler and constants (`FINAL`), the v2.8
  metric every version is judged on.

**Adoption gate, fixed before any result was seen:** a fusion is adopted only if its span tolerant mAP50 is above
v2.11's 0.0397 **and** neither class's pixel tolerant F1 falls, **and** the added inference fits the memory and
latency limits of §4.14.2.

Baseline (WireNet-R34 alone, the v2.11 configuration):

| | Strict / tolerant span mAP50 | Power tol. IoU / F1 | Telecom tol. IoU / F1 | Power→telecom | Telecom→power |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R34 | 0.0068 / **0.0397** | 0.528 / 0.792 | 0.422 / 0.677 | 0.018 | 0.045 |

##### Single models and every fusion on the 0.25 grid (`v212_ensemble3_val.jsonl`)

Every weight vector on the simplex, step 0.25 (15 vectors; R34 / MiT-B1 / ConvNeXt):

| R34 | MiT-B1 | ConvNeXt | Strict / tolerant span mAP50 | Power tol. F1 | Telecom tol. F1 | Telecom→power | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0 | 0 | 0.0068 / 0.0397 | 0.792 | 0.677 | 0.045 | baseline |
| 0 | 1 | 0 | 0.0065 / 0.0429 | 0.808 | 0.664 | 0.049 | fails (telecom F1) |
| 0 | 0 | 1 | 0.0064 / 0.0458 | 0.797 | 0.669 | 0.041 | fails (telecom F1) |
| 0.75 | 0.25 | 0 | 0.0064 / 0.0432 | 0.804 | 0.684 | 0.047 | passes |
| 0.5 | 0.5 | 0 | 0.0067 / 0.0392 | 0.815 | 0.685 | 0.046 | fails (span) |
| 0.25 | 0.75 | 0 | 0.0071 / 0.0497 | 0.814 | 0.674 | 0.048 | fails (telecom F1) |
| 0.75 | 0 | 0.25 | 0.0064 / 0.0447 | 0.802 | 0.681 | 0.046 | passes |
| 0.5 | 0 | 0.5 | 0.0063 / 0.0487 | 0.807 | 0.681 | 0.045 | passes |
| 0.25 | 0 | 0.75 | 0.0063 / 0.0462 | 0.804 | 0.676 | 0.043 | fails (telecom F1) |
| 0 | 0.75 | 0.25 | 0.0064 / 0.0468 | 0.813 | 0.671 | 0.047 | fails (telecom F1) |
| 0 | 0.5 | 0.5 | 0.0061 / 0.0487 | 0.815 | 0.680 | 0.042 | passes |
| 0 | 0.25 | 0.75 | 0.0062 / 0.0467 | 0.808 | 0.678 | 0.040 | passes |
| 0.5 | 0.25 | 0.25 | 0.0065 / 0.0455 | 0.813 | 0.687 | 0.046 | passes |
| 0.25 | 0.25 | 0.5 | 0.0065 / 0.0516 | 0.814 | 0.685 | 0.042 | passes |
| **0.25** | **0.5** | **0.25** | 0.0067 / **0.0537** | **0.819** | 0.684 | 0.045 | **passes, best** |

- **Every backbone alone beats R34 at span level** but loses telecom pixel F1. The fusion gets both.
- **Three models beat any pair**; R34 still contributes (its weight is non-zero in the best mix).
- **Strict mAP50 does not move** (0.0061–0.0071): the gain is in spans that are found within 2 px, not in exact
  masks, as in v2.8.
- **Power/telecom separation improves only slightly.** Power→telecom falls from 0.018 to 0.013 at best; telecom→
  power stays at 0.040–0.049. Fusion does not fix the class confusion between parallel bundles.
- The span score is not smooth on the grid (0.0392 at 0.5 / 0.5 / 0 between 0.0432 and 0.0497): with 22 photos,
  a few spans move it by several thousandths.

##### Is the gain larger than photo-to-photo noise? (`ensemble_bootstrap.py`, `v212_ensemble_bootstrap_val.json`)

The 22 photos were resampled with replacement 2,000 times (seed 0); in each resample every candidate and R34 are
scored on the same photos (paired), and tolerant mAP50 is recomputed exactly as the evaluator does (the full-sample
values are reproduced to four decimals).

| Candidate (R34 / MiT-B1 / ConvNeXt) | Tolerant mAP50 | 95% interval of the gain over R34 | Resamples where it beats R34 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.75 / 0.25 / 0 | 0.0432 | −0.0065 to +0.0126 | 76.9% |
| 0 / 0.5 / 0.5 | 0.0487 | −0.0057 to +0.0228 | 90.2% |
| 0.25 / 0.25 / 0.5 | 0.0516 | −0.0021 to +0.0276 | 95.0% |
| **0.25 / 0.5 / 0.25** | **0.0537** | −0.0039 to +0.0315 | **94.4%** |

The three-model gain is probable but not established: every interval still includes zero. Choosing the best of 15
grid points on the same 22 photos also makes the winner's score optimistic.

##### The fusion in the application (`app/pipeline/wirenet.py`, `models/v212_ensemble.json`)

`segmentation.wirenet_weights` may now name a JSON spec instead of a checkpoint:
`{"members": [{"weights": "models/wirenet-r34.pt", "weight": 0.25}, …]}`. `load_wirenet` then builds one module
that returns log(Σ wᵢ softmaxᵢ). The softmax every caller already applies gives back exactly the fused
probabilities, so flip TTA, dual polarity, the uncertainty summary and every calibration script work unchanged.

- **Equivalence.** Caching the 22 validation photos through the in-app fusion reproduces the offline fusion: the
  largest probability difference is 0.0005 (the float16 cache), the class maps agree on 99.998% of pixels, and span
  tolerant mAP50 is 0.0536 (offline 0.0537).
- **Latency** (`v212_latency_val.json`; full `run_analysis`, median of 2 after a warm-up, all 22 validation photos
  plus the dense test pole 145634; load average 2.5–3.4):

| | R34 (shipped) | Fusion (R34 + MiT-B1 + ConvNeXt, flip TTA) |
|---|---|---|
| Median per photo | 1.75 s | **4.71 s** |
| 90th percentile / slowest | 1.93 s / 1.99 s | 6.16 s / 6.54 s |
| Segmentation stage (median) | 1.37 s | 4.00 s |
| Dense pole (78 / 74 spans) | 1.82 s | **5.47 s** |
| Peak resident memory | 1.56 GB | 1.49 GB |

  One 1024² forward pass costs 0.20 s (R34), 0.58 s (MiT-B1), 0.69 s (ConvNeXt) and 1.49 s fused. Batching the
  plain and mirrored passes gives identical probabilities but no speed-up (0.42 / 1.16 / 1.65 s for batch 2): the
  M1 GPU is already saturated. A fused batch of 2 takes 11.7 s, because it no longer fits comfortably in GPU memory.
  Dark photos would pay double again (dual polarity).

##### Fusion without the mirror pass (`v212_ensemble3_notta_val.jsonl`)

Dropping flip TTA would bring the fusion back to roughly 2.6–2.7 s. Every model and every grid point was re-cached
and re-scored without the mirrored pass:

| R34 / MiT-B1 / ConvNeXt, single pass | Tolerant span mAP50 | Power tol. F1 | Telecom tol. F1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 / 0 / 0 | 0.0356 | 0.791 | 0.671 |
| 0 / 0.5 / 0.5 | 0.0503 | 0.814 | 0.677 |
| 0.25 / 0.25 / 0.5 | 0.0460 | 0.816 | 0.682 |
| 0.5 / 0.25 / 0.25 | 0.0453 | 0.812 | 0.684 |
| 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.25 | 0.0437 | 0.818 | 0.679 |

Single-pass fusions still beat the shipped R34 + flip, but by less (best 0.0503, whose telecom F1 only ties). They
also remove the mirrored pass that the reliability grade's disagreement term D comes from, and change the span
population from which the verdict's per-span false-alarm rate p0 was measured. Both would have to be re-calibrated
on validation, after some 30 configurations had already been compared on the same 22 photos.

##### Decision

**The fusion is not shipped.** It passes the accuracy gate, but not the latency limit of §4.14.2 (the v2.10 target
of 3.5 s for the densest pole), on the only hardware available, an 8 GB M1. The single-pass variant fits the time
limit but would need two re-calibrations on a validation set that has already been used for this selection.
v2.12 therefore ships **v2.11's pipeline unchanged**, and the fusion is kept as an opt-in configuration
(`wirenet_weights: models/v212_ensemble.json`) for faster hardware. On a machine where 4.7 s per photo is
acceptable, or with a faster GPU, it is the configuration to try first; its p0 and grade calibration should then be
re-checked on validation.

#### 4.14.5 Depth Anything V2 as a night-sky guard for YOLO vegetation (`depth_veg_guard.py`)

On the user's night pole P-D67F60, YOLO marks part of the black sky as Vegetation (score 0.27, 28,063 px). That
false instance creates "vegetation contact" hazards on telecom cables. **Depth Anything V2 Small** (Apache-2.0,
24.8 M parameters, run on the CPU so it does not compete with training) gives a relative depth map, and three
per-instance guards were tested:

- **G1 depth:** drop the instance if its median relative inverse depth is in the far (sky) range, below the photo's
  10th percentile + 0.05.
- **G2 photometric:** drop it if it is near-black and textureless, with mean L* < 15 and std L* < 4.
- **G3:** both G1 and G2.

| Guard | Validation instances removed | of which true vegetation | Vegetation AP50 (validation) | Night false instance removed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| none | 0 | 0 | 0.024 | no |
| G1 depth | 16 | **2** | **0.0025** | yes |
| G2 photometric | 0 | 0 | 0.024 | no (std L* 5.17) |
| G3 both | 0 | 0 | 0.024 | no |

G1 removes the night false positive but also real trees: on daytime photos, foliage against the sky is often
"far" in relative depth, and relative depth has no absolute scale to separate a tree 20 m away from the sky. G2
and G3 are safe on validation but miss the night case, whose sky is lit unevenly by street lamps.

A simpler rule, dropping any vegetation instance with mean L* < 15, was checked for safety on validation under
three conditions (`v212_veg_darkness_val.json`):

| Condition | Vegetation instances | Mean L* < 15 | of which true vegetation |
|---|---|---|---|
| clean | 127 | 7 | 0 |
| low light | 31 | 22 | **1** |
| synthetic night | 66 | 13 | 0 |

It removes a true tree in low light, and 22 of 31 low-light instances fall below the cut, so it is a darkness
detector, not a sky detector. **No guard is adopted.** The night false positive stays a documented limitation; the
real fix is labelled night vegetation (Figure `v212_night_veg_guard.jpg`).

#### 4.14.6 Depth and perspective in the sag measurement (`depth_sag.py`, `v212_depth_sag_val.json`)

The 666 validation spans scored with Chapter III's CRS (cached v2.8 probabilities, shipped constants) were paired
with Depth Anything V2's relative depth at the two span ends; **recession** = |depth(end A) − depth(end B)|.

- Recession and sag ratio are barely correlated overall: Spearman ρ = 0.063, p = 0.10.
- But the share of spans in the **high** CRS band rises with recession:

| Recession tercile (222 spans each) | High-band share | Median δ/L |
|---|---|---|
| low | 3.2% (7) | 0.0015 |
| middle | 7.2% (16) | 0.0017 |
| high | **10.4% (23)** | 0.0023 |

Low vs high tercile: Fisher's exact p = 0.0039; χ² across the three terciles p = 0.011.

**Reading.** Spans that run away from the camera read as high sag about 3.3× more often. This is consistent with
perspective: a straight wire receding in depth projects as a curve, which the image-space catenary reads as sag.
This matches the v2.9 audit, in which all 24 "service drops" were pole-to-pole spans seen in perspective (v2.9
§4.11.2a). **No correction is adopted.** Relative depth has no metric scale, there is no ground-truth sag to
calibrate against, and any re-weighting chosen here would be a new, unvalidated constant on top of the placeholder
δ_lim. The finding goes to the open problems: a perspective-aware sag needs metric depth, or two views, plus field
ground truth.

#### 4.14.7 Test split, evaluated once (`v212_spans_test.jsonl`)

v2.12 was frozen before the test split was touched: it ships v2.11's pipeline (WireNet-R34 + flip TTA + dual
polarity, YOLO vegetation, unchanged constants). The 21 test photos were then run once, under the same three
conditions as v2.11, with the full screener pipeline:

| Condition | Strict / tolerant mAP50 | Power tol. AP50 / R | Telecom tol. AP50 / R | vs v2.11 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| clean | 0.0204 / 0.0469 | 0.105 / 0.225 | 0.004 / 0.050 | identical |
| low light | 0.0053 / 0.0222 | 0.065 / 0.170 | 0.003 / 0.033 | identical |
| night (synthetic) | 0.0091 / 0.0313 | 0.088 / 0.217 | 0.001 / 0.025 | identical |

Every metric field of every row equals v2.11's `v211_spans_test.jsonl`; only the run time differs. The fusion, the
single backbones and the vegetation guards were **never run on the test split**, so the test split carries no
information about them and remains usable for a future v2.13 that ships one of them.


#### 4.14.8 Memory and stability on the 8 GB M1

- Two training processes were never run at the same time. ConvNeXt-Tiny started only after MiT-B1 had exited (a
  waiter on its PID).
- Depth Anything ran on the CPU, during training.
- Validation probability caching for each new model ran after its training, one model at a time.
- The live server was stopped during training and restarted cleanly at the end.

Measured: peak resident memory 1.31 GB while caching through the three-model fusion and 1.49 GB in the full
pipeline, with 68–70% of system memory free and no swapping during these runs. Only the batch-2 fused forward pass
(11.7 s) showed memory pressure; nothing in the shipped path uses it.

#### 4.14.9 Limitations

- Three backbones, one small dataset (102 training photos): the comparison says which backbone helps **here**, not
  in general.
- Batch 2 for the new backbones vs batch 4 for R34 is a confound imposed by memory.
- ConvNeXt-Tiny was trained for 20 epochs, not 40.
- Depth Anything gives relative, not metric, depth.

#### 4.14.10 Reproduction

```bash
python -m ml.wirenet train --encoder mit_b1 --batch 2 --epochs 40 --out models/v212_mitb1.pt
python -m ml.wirenet train --encoder tu-convnext_tiny --batch 2 --epochs 20 --out models/v212_convnext_t.pt
python -m ml.experiments.cache --split val --wirenet models/v212_mitb1.pt --tag=-mitb1
python -m ml.experiments.cache --split val --wirenet models/v212_convnext_t.pt --tag=-cnx
python -m ml.experiments.ensemble_eval --caches r34=runs/cache/val-v28 mitb1=runs/cache/val-mitb1 cnx=runs/cache/val-cnx --out docs/thesis/results/v212_ensemble_val.jsonl
python -m ml.experiments.depth_veg_guard --out docs/thesis/results/v212_veg_guard_val.json --night runs/ui_inputs/P-D67F60_night.jpg
python -m ml.experiments.depth_sag --out docs/thesis/results/v212_depth_sag_val.json
python -m ml.experiments.cache --split val --wirenet models/v212_ensemble.json --tag=-ens     # in-app equivalence
python -m ml.experiments.ensemble_bootstrap --caches r34=runs/cache/val-v28 mitb1=runs/cache/val-mitb1 cnx=runs/cache/val-cnx --out docs/thesis/results/v212_ensemble_bootstrap_val.json
for m in r34:models/wirenet-r34.pt mitb1:models/v212_mitb1.pt cnx:models/v212_convnext_t.pt; do
  python -m ml.experiments.cache --split val --wirenet ${m#*:} --tta none --tag=-${m%%:*}-notta; done
python -m ml.experiments.ensemble_eval --caches r34=runs/cache/val-r34-notta mitb1=runs/cache/val-mitb1-notta cnx=runs/cache/val-cnx-notta --out docs/thesis/results/v212_ensemble3_notta_val.jsonl
python -m ml.experiments.latency_profile --reps 2 --wirenet models/wirenet-r34.pt --images <22 val stems> 145634
python -m ml.experiments.latency_profile --reps 2 --wirenet models/v212_ensemble.json --images <22 val stems> 145634
python -m ml.experiments.pipeline_golden --check docs/thesis/results/v210_pipeline_golden_v29_val.json
python -m scripts.stress_test_all_conditions --split test --out docs/thesis/results/v212_spans_test.jsonl --conditions clean lowlight night --configs v2.12
```

The two-model fusion results are in `v212_ensemble2_val.jsonl` (R34 + MiT-B1, run before ConvNeXt finished;
identical to the corresponding rows of `v212_ensemble3_val.jsonl`). `v212_ensemble_val.jsonl` holds the R34
baseline row written while the evaluator was being set up.


---

## B.7 Report v2.13 (`docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.13_cv_ui.md`)

### Chapter 4 (continued) — v2.13: Ground-Structure Suppression, Field Guidance and the Field Screener Redesign

Branch `main`, from `4dbc6ca`. The shipped WireNet-R34 (`models/wirenet-r34.pt`, SHA-256 prefix `ab2353c7`) is
frozen: nothing in v2.13 retrains or modifies it, and YOLO11-seg (vegetation) and the Chapter III mathematics
(catenary fit, CRS, VNI) are unchanged. v2.13 works only before and after the model: pre-processing, a pretrained
scene gate and span post-processing. Every threshold was decided on the 22 validation photos, and a change was
adopted only if span-level tolerant mAP50 on validation stayed at or above the shipped **0.0397**. The 21 test
photos are run once, after v2.13 is frozen (§4.15.11).

**Hardware.** All official latency figures in this report were measured on the same **Apple Silicon M1 laptop
with 8 GB of unified memory** (PyTorch MPS) used since v2.6. The unit tests, the browser verification (§4.15.9) and
the documentation build for v2.13 were also run on a Windows 11 PC (Python 3.13.17, CPU only). Windows timings
are not comparable with the M1 and none appear in any latency table here.

| Mission | What was found | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| Ground gate (pretrained scene parsing) | UperNet-ConvNeXt-T (ADE20K, MIT licence) labels road / sidewalk / floor / earth / … pixels; a span lying ≥ 50% on them is a kerb, manhole or pavement edge. Validation: removes 0 spans (tolerant mAP50 unchanged, 0.0397). Live problem photo: removes the road and manhole traces, including a false CRS 8.6 span; verdict AT RISK – HIGH → NOT AT RISK. | **Adopted** (`ground_gate: true`) |
| Field guidance | One `action` per unmeasured or edge-truncated span, and one photo-level `retake_guidance` when ≥ 40% of the spans could not be measured. Derived from photo size, span position and failure code only. | **Adopted** |
| Always-on CLAHE on L* | Tolerant mAP50 0.0397 → 0.0356 (strict 0.0068 → 0.0117). | Rejected |
| Oriented black-hat tubeness blend | β = 0.25 / 0.5 / 1 / 2: 0.0387 / 0.0367 / 0.0280 / 0.0336. | Rejected |
| Span bridging (gap / angle) | 12° variants 0.0357–0.0381; 60 px / 25° 0.0407 (within noise of the shipped 70 px / 25°). | Shipped 70 px / 25° kept |
| Closed-loop, isolated-below-network, upward-bulge rules | Loop and isolation rules remove no labelled wire on validation but remove real wires on unlabelled street photos (labels are incomplete); bulge rules remove labelled wires. | Rejected |
| Field screener redesign (`/workspace`) | Dark theme, Geist, gold accent, glass panels, hero verdict card, view toggles + opacity, collapsed diagnostics, 200–300 ms motion, shimmer loader. Browser-verified in 16 scenario × theme × viewport combinations; one phone-width bug found and fixed. | **Shipped** |
| Latency (M1) | Gate adds 0.26 s when it runs; a noisy first profile showed larger jumps on two photos. | **[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]** |

---

#### 4.15.1 The failure: WireNet traces the ground

WireNet-R34 segments thin, dark, elongated structures. In the training crops these are almost always conductors
against the sky. In a live street photo taken from pavement level, the lower half of the frame also contains thin
dark lines: kerb edges, manhole rims, pavement joints, road markings and railings. WireNet traces them, the span
extractor turns them into "spans", and the catenary fit scores them. Because such a trace is rarely a clean
hanging curve, it can come out as a strongly sagging span with a high CRS.

The v2.13 problem photo (`images (1).jpeg`, 452 × 678 px, a live upload without labels) showed exactly this: road
and manhole traces in the bottom of the frame, one of them scored at **CRS 8.6**, drove the verdict to
**AT RISK – HIGH** although no overhead conductor in the photo was sagging.

No retraining was allowed, so the remedy had to come from outside the model: (a) image pre-processing that makes
real wires easier to separate (§4.15.4), (b) a pretrained model that knows where the ground is (§4.15.2), or
(c) geometric rules on the extracted spans (§4.15.5–4.15.6).

#### 4.15.2 Ground gate (`app/pipeline/scene.py`, `HybridSegmenter._ground_gate`)

**Model choice.** A semantic scene parser trained on ADE20K (150 classes, including *road*, *sidewalk*, *floor*,
*earth*, *path*, *grass*, *field*, *sand*, *dirt track*, *runway*, *rug*) labels every pixel. Two pretrained parsers
were considered:

| Candidate | Licence | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| SegFormer-B0 (ADE20K) | NVIDIA Source Code Licence: non-commercial, research only | Rejected: incompatible with a deployable screener |
| UperNet + ConvNeXt-Tiny (`openmmlab/upernet-convnext-tiny`, 60 M parameters) | MIT | **Adopted** |

**Rule.** For every extracted wire span, the share of its mask pixels that lie on ground classes is computed
(`ground_share`). A span with `ground_share ≥ ground_gate_share` (**0.5**) is not scored. It is returned in the
API as an entry of `suppressed_spans` with its class, confidence, bbox, polygons, ground share, `reason_code:
"ground_structure"` and the plain-language reason *"Lies on the road or pavement: a kerb, manhole or pavement
edge, not an overhead wire"*. The result also carries the flag `ground_structures_suppressed`. Overhead
conductors are seen against sky, buildings or trees, so a span lying mostly on the road surface is not an
overhead conductor.

**Resolution and cost.** UperNet's pyramid pooling (bins 1, 2, 3, 6) needs a feature map divisible by 6 on MPS;
at 384 px the stride-32 map is 12 × 12. On the M1 the parser runs in **0.26 s at 384 px** (0.54 s at 576 px), and
its ground mask agrees with the 576 px mask on **98.8%** of the problem photo's pixels.

**Skip rule.** Ground lies below the wires in a pole photo. When no span's bounding box reaches the lower half of
the frame, the parser is not run at all (output identical, 0.26 s saved).

**Fail-open.** If the parser cannot load (no network on first run, missing `transformers`), every span is kept and
the result is flagged `ground_gate_unavailable`. The gate never blocks a screening.

**Configuration.** `config/wireframe.yaml`: `ground_gate: true`, `ground_gate_share: 0.5` (defaults in
`app/core/config.py` keep the gate off, so older configurations reproduce exactly).

#### 4.15.3 Ground gate: validation and the problem photo

**Validation (`v213_filters_val.json`, 828 spans, 96 true-positive spans).** The validation photos are crops of
the wire region and contain no ground, so the gate removes nothing:

| Rule | Spans removed | True-positive spans removed | Tolerant mAP50 |
|---|---|---|---|
| none (shipped v2.12) | — | — | 0.0397 |
| ground share ≥ 0.5 (**adopted**) | 0 | 0 | 0.0397 |
| ground share ≥ 0.8 | 0 | 0 | 0.0397 |

The adoption rule (validation tolerant mAP50 ≥ 0.0397) is met with equality. The whole-pipeline golden check that
confirms the validation outputs are byte-identical with the gate on is **[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]** (§4.15.11).

**Problem photo (unlabelled, M1 session).** With the gate on, the road and manhole traces are suppressed,
including the false CRS 8.6 span, and the verdict changes from **AT RISK – HIGH** to **NOT AT RISK**. The traces stay
visible in the diagnostics drawer under "Ground structures ignored", so a reviewer can see what was removed and why.
Because the photo has no labels, this is a visual check, not a metric. The figure-generation script is
`ml/experiments/v213_unlabelled.py`.

#### 4.15.4 Pre-processing candidates (`ml/experiments/v213_enhance.py`, `v213_enhance_val.jsonl`)

Both candidates aim to raise the contrast of thin dark structures before WireNet sees the photo. Both were applied
to every validation photo; neither is adopted.

| Variant | Extra time | Strict mAP50 | Tolerant mAP50 | Spans | Power recall | Telecom recall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| shipped | — | 0.0068 | **0.0397** | 828 | 0.213 | 0.088 |
| CLAHE on L*, always on | 21.6 ms | 0.0117 | 0.0356 | 841 | 0.178 | 0.091 |
| black-hat blend β = 0.25 | 48.9 ms | 0.0068 | 0.0387 | 826 | 0.197 | 0.094 |
| black-hat blend β = 0.5 | 48.9 ms | 0.0062 | 0.0367 | 836 | 0.204 | 0.091 |
| black-hat blend β = 1 | 48.9 ms | 0.0062 | 0.0280 | 853 | 0.159 | 0.079 |
| black-hat blend β = 2 | 48.9 ms | 0.0064 | 0.0336 | 888 | 0.201 | 0.073 |

CLAHE raises strict mAP50 (exact-boundary matches) but lowers the tolerant metric that the adoption rule uses, and
lowers power recall. WireNet was trained on unequalised photos, so equalisation moves the input away from its
training distribution. The oriented black-hat (tubeness) blend loses at every strength. The v2.10 conditional
enhancement routing is unchanged.

#### 4.15.5 Span bridging (`v213_bridging_val.json`)

Fragmented spans could be healed by a looser collinear gap bridge. The shipped extractor bridges gaps up to
`span_max_gap` = 70 px within `span_gap_angle_deg` = 25° (v2.8).

| Max gap | Angle | Tolerant mAP50 | Strict mAP50 | Spans | Power recall | Telecom recall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **70 px** | **25°** (shipped) | **0.0397** | 0.0068 | 828 | 0.213 | 0.088 |
| 60 px | 25° | 0.0407 | 0.0067 | 831 | 0.213 | 0.094 |
| 100 px | 25° | 0.0378 | 0.0068 | 795 | 0.210 | 0.079 |
| 60 px | 12° | 0.0367 | 0.0067 | 859 | 0.197 | 0.097 |
| 70 px | 12° | 0.0379 | 0.0068 | 862 | 0.204 | 0.094 |
| 100 px | 12° | 0.0381 | 0.0068 | 855 | 0.210 | 0.094 |
| 150 px | 12° | 0.0357 | 0.0069 | 845 | 0.197 | 0.094 |

A stricter 12° angle loses at every gap. 60 px / 25° is +0.001, three spans different from the shipped setting,
which is within photo-to-photo noise on 22 photos (v2.12 §4.14.4). Changing a shipped parameter for a gain of that
size after this many tries would be selection on noise, so **70 px / 25° is kept**.

#### 4.15.6 Geometric span rules (`ml/experiments/v213_filters.py`, `v213_filters_val.json`)

Per-span features were logged for all 828 validation spans (`v213_span_features_val.jsonl`): ground share, number
of enclosed holes in the span mask, upward bulge, quadratic-fit residual, length, isolation from other wires and
distance below the wire network. Each candidate rule was then applied:

| Rule | Removed | True-positive removed | Tolerant mAP50 |
|---|---|---|---|
| closed loop (≥ 1 enclosed hole) | 16 | 0 | 0.0398 |
| isolated ≥ 40 px and ≥ 40 px below the network | 2 | 0 | 0.0397 |
| isolated ≥ 80 px and ≥ 80 px below the network | 2 | 0 | 0.0397 |
| upward bulge ≥ 3 px and ≥ 2% | 82 | 2 | 0.0388 |
| upward bulge ≥ 5 px and ≥ 4% | 37 | 1 | 0.0393 |

The bulge rules remove labelled wires and are rejected on validation. The loop and isolation rules remove no
labelled wire and do not lower the metric. On the unlabelled street photos (the problem photo, the night pole and
the demo samples, `v213_unlabelled.py`), however, they **removed real wires**: service drops and isolated low
conductors that the validation labels do not include. The validation labels are incomplete for exactly the kind
of wire these rules target, so "0 true positives removed" is not evidence that the rules are safe. Both are
**rejected**.

#### 4.15.7 Known limitation: fence and railing traces

One railing trace on the problem photo (span #18) is not removed. ADE20K has a *fence* / *railing* class, but at
the parser's resolution the railing in front of the facade is labelled *building*. A rule on *fence* would therefore
not fire here, and a rule on *building* would remove real cables seen against facades. No cue was found that
separates the railing from a cable without also removing real cables. The trace is **unmeasured** (it fails the
catenary checks), so it appears in the span list with its reason but does not affect the verdict. Removing such
traces needs a model that sees them as non-wires, which is retraining and out of scope for v2.13.

#### 4.15.8 Field guidance (`app/services/reasons.py`)

v2.11 gave every unmeasured span a reason. v2.13 adds what the operator can do about it:

- **Per span (`wire_action`).** For an unmeasured span with a short chord (`chord_below_precision_gate`,
  `too_short`) on a photo under 1000 px on its short side, the action is to upload the full-resolution photo, naming
  its size (e.g. *"Upload the full-resolution photo (this one is 452 px on its short side), or move closer so the
  span looks longer"*). On a full-resolution photo it is "move back or centre the span" if the span touches the
  frame edge, otherwise "move closer or zoom in". A span that does not follow one curve (`residual_too_large`,
  `non_positive_a`) gets "shoot from a few metres along the street, where this wire does not cross others"; a fit
  that did not converge or is degenerate gets "shoot the span from the side". A scored span touching the frame
  edge gets "step back so both poles of this span are in the frame". Service drops get no action.
- **Per photo (`retake_summary`).** When ≥ 40% of the non-drop spans could not be measured, the result carries one
  `retake_guidance` sentence: *"N of M spans could not be measured. Best next photo: …"*, using the most common
  span action. The field screener shows it as the single next action.

The guidance is derived from photo size, span position and failure code only. It never changes a measurement or a
verdict.

#### 4.15.9 Field screener redesign and browser verification

**Design.** `/workspace` (`app/web/field.html`, `assets/field.css`, `assets/field.js`) was rebuilt on the landing
page's design tokens: dark by default (light one tap away, for direct sunlight), Geist / Geist Mono, gold accent,
glass panels. The result screen is ordered for a two-second read:

1. a **hero verdict card**: status pill (**HAZARD DETECTED**, **REVIEW REQUIRED**, **NOT AT RISK**,
   **RETAKE PHOTO**, display labels for the unchanged API verdicts), worst CRS, spans measured out of total, and
   **one next action**;
2. the photo with an **Original / Detected spans / Full wire mask** segmented control and an **overlay opacity
   slider** (`OverlayViewer.setOpacity`: overlays fade toward the photo as photo · (1 − o) + overlaid · o);
3. every span grouped by urgency, each unmeasured one with its reason and action, linked both ways to the canvas;
4. a **collapsed diagnostics drawer** for engineers and reviewers: sag corroboration, photo reliability (entropy,
   flip disagreement, L*, contrast, noise), WireNet mask coverage, ground structures ignored, timings, weights and
   configuration fingerprint.

State changes animate in 200–300 ms, and a shimmer skeleton of the result layout is shown while the analysis runs,
so nothing jumps when it arrives. `prefers-reduced-motion` disables the motion.

**Verification method (`scripts/ui_check.py --mock`).** The detection models were not on the verification PC.
The check therefore uploads the bundled synthetic sample with its reference polygons once, to obtain a genuine
`/result` payload from the running server. It then derives four scenarios from it: HAZARD DETECTED (two spans in
the high band), REVIEW REQUIRED (the sample's own verdict), NOT AT RISK with two `suppressed_spans`, and RETAKE
PHOTO (three spans with `chord_below_precision_gate`, their actions and the photo-level `retake_guidance`).
Playwright intercepts `/api/health`, the upload, the status poll (held at *processing* for two polls so the loader
is observed), `/result`, the wire-mask PNG and the PDF. Each scenario was checked in Chrome in the dark and the
light theme, at 1440 × 900 and at 390 × 844 (phone, touch).

**Result (`results/ui_check_v213.json`): 16 / 16 combinations pass**, plus the check that the two themes resolve to
different token sets (body background `rgb(12, 13, 15)` dark, `rgb(243, 242, 239)` light). In every combination:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Console errors and uncaught page errors | 0 |
| Shimmer skeleton running (`fx-shimmer`) and `aria-busy` while processing | yes |
| Status pill text matches the scenario | 16 / 16 |
| Worst CRS and spans measured / total KPIs; one next action | yes; RETAKE: next action = `retake_guidance` |
| `[data-view]` Original / Detected spans / Full wire mask switch the viewer layers; full mask image loads | yes |
| `#opacity` sets the viewer opacity (40 → 0.40, label "40%"); marked off in Original | yes |
| `#diag` collapsed by default; opens (`aria-expanded="true"`, height > 40 px) and closes | yes |
| Every unmeasured span shows a reason | yes |
| Suppressed traces listed in diagnostics ("94% on road / pavement"); KPI "2 ground traces ignored" | yes |
| Transition / animation durations: drawer 280 ms, view thumb 260 ms, opacity control 200 ms, stage 280 ms | within 200–300 ms |
| Table → canvas and canvas → table selection (desktop) | yes |
| Horizontal page scroll; span list fits its panel; filters hide rows | none; fits; yes |

**Bug found and fixed.** At 390 px the spans table was wider than its panel and scrolled sideways inside it,
clipping each span's reason and next step (the text a field operator most needs). Below 520 px each span is now a
card: id, conductor and CRS on one line, then status, reason and action at full width. The checker gained two
checks (`spans_table_fits`, `filter_hides_rows`) that would have caught it.

Figures: `figures/v213_ui_hazard_dark_desktop.jpg`, `v213_ui_not_at_risk_light_desktop.jpg`,
`v213_ui_retake_dark_phone.jpg`, `v213_ui_loader_dark_desktop.jpg`.

The mocked check verifies the interface, not the models. The real-photo mode of the same script
(`python -m scripts.ui_check --base … photos…`) is to be re-run on the M1 with the models installed.

#### 4.15.10 Latency on the 8 GB M1 (`ml/experiments/latency_profile.py`)

Target for v2.13: under 3.0 s per photo on the M1. A first profile, gate off → on, was taken while the machine was
under load (load average 5.8):

| Photo set | Gate off | Gate on |
|---|---|---|
| Validation, median | 1.73 s | 2.29 s |
| Validation, p90 | 1.91 s | 2.53 s |
| Validation, max | 2.21 s | 2.72 s |
| Dense pole | 1.97 s | 2.53 s |
| Problem photo | 1.69 s | 2.13 s |
| Night pole | 2.29 s | 4.23 s |
| Demo photo 2 | 1.98 s | 3.24 s |

The parser itself costs 0.26 s when it runs. The validation median rose by 0.56 s and the night pole by 1.94 s,
much more than that, which points to contention from the concurrent load rather than to the gate. These numbers
are therefore **not** used to claim that the target is met or missed. The quiet re-measurement (no other process
running, same photos, gate off and on) is **[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]**, and its raw output goes to
`results/v213_latency_val.json`. Until then the night pole (4.23 s) and demo photo 2 (3.24 s) remain open against the
3.0 s target.

#### 4.15.11 Golden check and the test split

- **Whole-pipeline golden check, validation, gate on**:
  `python -m ml.experiments.pipeline_golden --check docs/thesis/results/v210_pipeline_golden_v29_val.json`.
  Expected: 22 / 22 identical (the gate removes no validation span). **[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]**
- **Test split, once, frozen v2.13** (`v2.13` config in `scripts/stress_test_all_conditions.py`: the shipped
  configuration, i.e. v2.12 plus the ground gate):
  `python -m scripts.stress_test_all_conditions --split test --conditions clean lowlight night --configs v2.13 --out docs/thesis/results/v213_spans_test.jsonl`.
  **[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]**

Neither run was possible on the verification PC: the dataset (`datasets/wireframe_seg/`) and the WireNet checkpoint
are not stored there, and the test split must be run exactly once on the frozen configuration.

#### 4.15.12 Software verification

On Windows 11, Python 3.13.17, CPU: **508 passed, 4 skipped** (the four skips need `ultralytics` /
`albumentations` from `requirements-ml.txt`), and `ruff check app ml scripts tests` is clean. New tests:

- `tests/test_v213_ground_gate.py` (11 tests): a fake scene parser (offline, no weight download); suppression and the
  recorded fields; inclusive 0.5 threshold; gate off; lower-half skip; fail-open when the parser is unavailable;
  load failure reported; shipped configuration.
- `tests/test_v213_reasons.py` (25 tests): `touches_edge`, every `wire_action` branch, `retake_summary` (40%
  threshold, service drops, most common action), and through the API: `suppressed_spans`, the
  `ground_structures_suppressed` / `ground_gate_unavailable` flags, that suppressed traces are never listed as wires,
  per-span `action` and `retake_guidance`.
- `tests/test_v213_viewer_opacity.py` (8 tests): Node evaluates `viewer.js`; `setOpacity` clamps to [0, 1] and
  redraws; the photo is repainted with alpha 1 − o only below full opacity, with overlays on and a photo loaded.
- `tests/test_v211_field_ui.py`: the console asset check now asserts the v2.13 redesign.

#### 4.15.13 Limitations

- The ground gate is verified on validation only in the sense that it changes nothing there; its benefit was shown
  on unlabelled live photos. A labelled set of full street-level photos, with ground in the frame, would be needed
  to measure how many false spans it removes and whether it ever removes a real low conductor (for example a
  service drop crossing in front of the road).
- Fence and railing traces are not removed (§4.15.7).
- The parser adds a 60 M-parameter model and 0.26 s when a span reaches the lower half of the frame. The latency
  target is open until the quiet M1 run (§4.15.10).
- The rejected loop and isolation rules show that validation labels are incomplete for low, isolated wires, so
  "no true positive removed" on validation is weak evidence for any suppression rule.
- The browser verification used mocked results derived from one real payload; it verifies the interface, not the
  detections.

#### 4.15.14 Reproduction

```bash
pip install -r requirements-dev.txt playwright           # tests, ruff, UI check
python -m pytest
ruff check app ml scripts tests
uvicorn app.main:app --port 8765 &                        # any server; models not required for --mock
python -m scripts.ui_check --mock --base http://127.0.0.1:8765 --shots runs/ui_shots_v213 \
    --out docs/thesis/results/ui_check_v213.json
# On the M1 with the dataset and models (requirements-ml.txt):
python -m ml.experiments.v213_enhance --out docs/thesis/results/v213_enhance_val.jsonl
python -m ml.experiments.v213_filters --out docs/thesis/results/v213_span_features_val.jsonl
python -m ml.experiments.v213_filters --analyse docs/thesis/results/v213_span_features_val.jsonl \
    --summary docs/thesis/results/v213_filters_val.json
python -m ml.experiments.v213_unlabelled --images runs/v213_inputs/*.jpg runs/demo_samples/* \
    --out docs/thesis/results/v213_unlabelled.json --figures docs/thesis/figures
# quiet machine, nothing else running; gate off, then on
python -m ml.experiments.latency_profile --images <stems or paths> --reps 5 --no-ground-gate --out runs/lat_off.json
python -m ml.experiments.latency_profile --images <stems or paths> --reps 5 --out runs/lat_on.json
python -m ml.experiments.pipeline_golden --check docs/thesis/results/v210_pipeline_golden_v29_val.json
python -m scripts.stress_test_all_conditions --split test --conditions clean lowlight night --configs v2.13 \
    --out docs/thesis/results/v213_spans_test.jsonl      # once
```

# Part C — Appendices


## C.1 Complete disclosure log (v2.1 to v2.13, including the v2.6 baseline)

### WIREFRAME v2: disclosure log

Branch `refactor/chapter-iii-v2`, built from `main` at `90e1bc4`. This log records what changed, why, what
was verified and how, and what is still open. Development models were trained from v2.6 onwards and evaluated on
the test split as each version's report records (the evaluation results themselves are in `docs/thesis/`); none of
the Table 3.10 targets is met. (Until v2.12 this paragraph still read as written for v2.1, that no evaluation result
was claimed and no Table 3.10 target had been measured on test; it was out of date from v2.6 and is corrected here.)

---

#### v2.13: ground-structure gate, field guidance, field screener redesign (branch `main`)

Full methods and tables: `docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.13_cv_ui.md`. The shipped WireNet-R34 (`ab2353c7`) is frozen and
was not retrained or modified; YOLO11-seg and the Chapter III mathematics (catenary fit, CRS, VNI) are unchanged.
Every threshold was decided on the 22 validation photos; a change was adopted only if validation span tolerant
mAP50 stayed at or above the shipped 0.0397.

**Hardware.** Official latency figures: the 8 GB Apple M1 (MPS), as for every version since v2.6. The unit tests,
the browser verification and the documentation build were also run on a Windows 11 PC (Python 3.13.17, CPU only);
no Windows timing is reported as, or mixed with, an M1 latency figure.

##### Added
* **Ground gate** (`app/pipeline/scene.py`, `HybridSegmenter._ground_gate`; config `segmentation.ground_gate: true`,
  `ground_gate_share: 0.5`). UperNet-ConvNeXt-T trained on ADE20K (`openmmlab/upernet-convnext-tiny`, MIT licence,
  60 M parameters) labels ground classes at 384 px (0.26 s on the M1; 98.8% agreement with 576 px on the problem
  photo). A span with ≥ 50% of its pixels on road / sidewalk / floor / earth / path / grass / field / sand / dirt
  track / runway / rug is not scored and is returned in `suppressed_spans` with its reason; the result is flagged
  `ground_structures_suppressed`. The parser is skipped when no span reaches the lower half of the frame, and the
  gate fails open (`ground_gate_unavailable`) when the parser cannot load. SegFormer-B0 was rejected because of its
  NVIDIA research-only licence.
* **Field guidance** (`app/services/reasons.py`): per-span `action` (`wire_action`) and photo-level
  `retake_guidance` (`retake_summary`, when ≥ 40% of the non-drop spans are unmeasured).
* **Field screener redesign** (`app/web/field.html`, `assets/field.css`, `assets/field.js`; `viewer.js`
  `setOpacity`): dark theme by default with light toggle, Geist, gold accent, glass panels; hero verdict card
  (HAZARD DETECTED / REVIEW REQUIRED / NOT AT RISK / RETAKE PHOTO, worst CRS, measured vs total spans, one next
  action); Original / Detected spans / Full wire mask control with an overlay opacity slider; diagnostics in a
  collapsed drawer; 200–300 ms transitions; shimmer loader.
* `scripts/ui_check.py --mock` (four scenarios from one genuine `/result` payload, Playwright routing), the `v2.13`
  config in `scripts/stress_test_all_conditions.py`, `--no-ground-gate` in `latency_profile`,
  `ml/experiments/v213_enhance.py`, `v213_filters.py`, `v213_unlabelled.py`.
* `transformers` moved into the runtime `requirements.txt` (it was listed only in `requirements-ml.txt` as a v2.12
  experiment dependency, so a runtime-only install would have left the adopted gate permanently unavailable).

##### Tested and rejected (all on validation)
* **Always-on CLAHE on L\***: tolerant mAP50 0.0397 -> 0.0356 (strict 0.0068 -> 0.0117).
* **Oriented black-hat tubeness blend**: β = 0.25 / 0.5 / 1 / 2 gave 0.0387 / 0.0367 / 0.0280 / 0.0336
  (`v213_enhance_val.jsonl`).
* **12° bridging**: 0.0357–0.0381 (60 px / 25° gave 0.0407, within noise; 70 px / 25° kept).
* **Closed-loop and isolated-below-network rules**: removed 0 labelled wires on validation but removed real wires on
  unlabelled street photos (validation labels are incomplete for such wires).
* **Upward-bulge rules**: removed labelled wires (1–2 true-positive spans).

##### Known limitations
* A fence-rail trace on the problem photo (#18) is not removed: the scene parser labels the railing as building, and
  no cue separates railings from real cables in front of facades. The trace is unmeasured and does not affect the
  verdict.
* The gate's benefit is shown on unlabelled live photos only; the validation crops contain no ground.
* A first M1 latency profile under load (load average 5.8) showed gate-on jumps larger than the 0.26 s parser cost
  (night pole 2.29 -> 4.23 s, demo photo 2 1.98 -> 3.24 s). The < 3.0 s target is open until a quiet re-run.

##### Verified
* Windows, Python 3.13.17: **508 passed, 4 skipped** (skips need `ultralytics` / `albumentations`); scoped ruff
  clean. New: `tests/test_v213_ground_gate.py` (fake scene parser, offline), `tests/test_v213_reasons.py` (guidance
  and the API's `suppressed_spans`, flags, `action`, `retake_guidance`), `tests/test_v213_viewer_opacity.py` (Node).
* Browser (Chrome, `--mock`): 16 / 16 scenario × theme × viewport combinations pass, zero console errors
  (`docs/thesis/results/ui_check_v213.json`).
* **Bug found and fixed**: at 390 px the spans table scrolled sideways inside its panel and clipped each span's
  reason and retake instruction; below 520 px each span is now a card.
* Ground gate on validation: 0 spans removed, tolerant mAP50 unchanged at 0.0397 (`v213_filters_val.json`).

##### Pending (M1 with the dataset and models)
* **[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]** latency re-measurement, gate off / on, on a quiet machine.
* **[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]** whole-pipeline golden check with the gate on
  (`--check docs/thesis/results/v210_pipeline_golden_v29_val.json`).
* **[PENDING QUIET M1 RUN]** the test split, once, on frozen v2.13 (`--configs v2.13`).
* The real-photo mode of `scripts/ui_check.py` with the models installed.

#### v2.12: complementary backbones, probability fusion, monocular depth (branch `v2.12-multi-model-ensemble`)

Full methods and tables: `docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.12_ensemble.md`. The shipped WireNet-R34 (`ab2353c7`),
`v2.6-baseline`, `7867d56`, `dc1177b`, `5989657`, `7cdb244` and `eff526a` are untouched. New checkpoints are
saved separately as `models/v212_*.pt`.

**Hardware.** Every training, evaluation and latency run in this log from v2.6 onwards was executed locally on one
Apple Silicon M1 laptop with 8 GB of unified memory (MPS GPU backend, shared CPU/GPU memory). No cloud GPU was
used. The memory limit decided which backbones could be trained (MiT-B2 ran out of memory at batch 4), the batch
size (2 for the new backbones) and that only one heavy process ran at a time.

##### Added
* `--encoder` / `--arch` in the WireNet trainer and an `.arch.json` sidecar per checkpoint, which the application
  reads (`app/pipeline/wirenet.py: model_arch`); files without a sidecar load as ResNet-34 U-Net, as before.
* `ml.experiments.cache --wirenet` to cache any candidate's validation probabilities.
* `ml/experiments/ensemble_eval.py` (weighted probability fusion; pixel and span scoring),
  `depth_veg_guard.py`, `depth_sag.py`, `ensemble_bootstrap.py`; `transformers` 4.57.6 (added to `requirements-ml.txt`) for Depth Anything V2 Small (Apache-2.0); `--tta` in `ml.experiments.cache`, `--wirenet` in `latency_profile`; the `v2.12` config in `scripts/stress_test_all_conditions.py`.
* **Ensemble specs**: `segmentation.wirenet_weights` may name a JSON spec (`models/v212_ensemble.json`); `load_wirenet` then builds a probability-fusion module. The shipped config is unchanged.
* `scripts/build_master_manuscript.py` and `docs/thesis/MASTER_CHAPTER4_MANUSCRIPT.md`.

##### Trained (kept as separate files, not shipped)
* **MiT-B1 U-Net** (`models/v212_mitb1.pt`, SHA-256 `f595ef68…`): 40 epochs, batch 2, 4.66 h; best epoch 32.
* **ConvNeXt-Tiny U-Net** (`models/v212_convnext_t.pt`, `9633cee8…`): 20 epochs, batch 2, 3.39 h; best epoch 16.
  Standard recipe (one-cycle, end-to-end, validation after every epoch). A 14-epoch progressive-unfreezing schedule
  was queued briefly and cancelled before it ran; its code was removed.
* Neither weight file is committed (66 MB and 128 MB); logs and `.arch.json` sidecars are.

##### Tested, passed the accuracy gate, not shipped
* **Three-model probability fusion** (R34 0.25 / MiT-B1 0.5 / ConvNeXt 0.25, `models/v212_ensemble.json`).
  Validation span tolerant mAP50 0.0397 -> 0.0537; power / telecom tolerant F1 0.792 / 0.677 -> 0.819 / 0.684. It
  beats R34 in 94.4% of 2,000 paired bootstrap resamples, but every 95% interval includes zero.
  **Not shipped**: 4.71 s median per photo and 5.47 s on the dense pole (R34: 1.75 s / 1.82 s), over the 3.5 s
  limit. Memory was stable (peak 1.49 GB). Kept as an opt-in configuration; the user chose this option.
* **Single-pass fusion** (no flip TTA): fits the time limit but gains less (best 0.0503) and would require
  re-calibrating p0 and the reliability grade, which were measured with R34 + flip. Not adopted.
* **v2.12 ships v2.11's pipeline**: whole-pipeline golden check 22/22 identical; `wirenet-r34.pt` still `ab2353c7`.

##### Tested and rejected
* **MiT-B2 and SegFormer-B2.** MiT-B2 ran out of memory at batch 4; SegFormer-B2's 2.31 s per 1024² inference does
  not fit the latency budget. HRNet-W18 was probed but not trained (one ConvNet was budgeted; ConvNeXt was chosen).
* **Depth gate for YOLO vegetation (G1).** It removes the night-sky false positive on P-D67F60, but also 2 true
  vegetation instances on validation; vegetation AP50 0.024 -> 0.0025.
* **Photometric gates (G2: mean L* < 15 and std < 4; G3: G1 and G2).** No validation loss, but they miss the night
  false positive (std L* 5.17).
* **Darkness-only rule (mean L* < 15).** It removes a true tree in low light and 22/31 low-light instances: unsafe.
* **Perspective correction of sag.** Not attempted: high-band share rises with depth recession (3.2% -> 10.4%,
  Fisher p = 0.0039), but relative depth has no metric scale and there is no ground-truth sag. Reported as a
  finding only.

##### Known limitations
* The YOLO night-sky vegetation false positive remains.
* Image-space sag is perspective-sensitive (above).
* The new backbones were trained at batch 2, not 4; ConvNeXt-Tiny for 20 epochs, not 40.

##### Verified
* 462 tests pass (10 new in `tests/test_v212_ensemble.py`: arch sidecar, ensemble spec validation, fused softmax =
  weighted mean of member probabilities, shipped config still single R34); scoped ruff is clean.
* In-app fusion reproduces the offline fusion on validation (max probability difference 0.0005, 99.998% pixel
  agreement, tolerant mAP50 0.0536 vs 0.0537).
* Whole-pipeline golden check of the shipped configuration: identical on 22/22 validation photos.
* Test split, run once on the frozen v2.12 (`v212_spans_test.jsonl`): clean 0.0204 / 0.0469, low light
  0.0053 / 0.0222, synthetic night 0.0091 / 0.0313, every metric field identical to v2.11. No fusion, backbone or
  vegetation guard was run on test.
* Housekeeping: six stale background wait loops from earlier sessions (their `pgrep` patterns matched themselves)
  were found and stopped; they ran no computation.

#### v2.11: dual-polarity wire detection, full-mask exposure, field inspection console (branch `v2.11-field-ui`)

Full methods and tables: `docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.11_field_ui.md`. No model was retrained and no new checkpoint
was made. WireNet (`ab2353c7`), `v2.6-baseline`, `7867d56`, `dc1177b`, `5989657` and `7cdb244` are untouched.

##### Accepted
* **Dual-polarity WireNet inference** (`segmentation.dual_polarity`). On dark photos (mean L* <= 40) WireNet also
  runs on the photo with L* inverted, and the inverted pass is used if it finds >= 1.2x the wire pixels. Root
  cause: at night conductors are lighter than the sky, but every training photo has dark wires on a bright sky.
  - Validation: 0 of 176 daytime cases switch, and clean and low light are byte-identical (golden check 22/22).
  - Synthetic night (validation): tolerant mAP50 0.0051 -> 0.0312.
  - The user's dense night pole P-D67F60: 12 -> 27 spans (8 -> 18 measured); wire pixels 9,694 -> 23,464.
* **Everything the model detected is exposed.** The full WireNet class mask is served at
  `/api/analysis/{id}/wiremask.png` with coverage statistics. Every unmeasured span carries a reason code and a
  plain-language reason (`app/services/reasons.py`); per-span vegetation clearance, telemetry and the corroboration
  parameters are in the API.
* **`/workspace` rebuilt as an inspection console:**
  - a daylight slate theme scoped to the field page;
  - a 58/42 viewer and detail split, with layer toggles and zoom;
  - a telemetry bar;
  - a retake banner, then hazards, then the verdict and KPIs;
  - an urgency-grouped span table, linked both ways to the canvas;
  - collapsed diagnostics;
  - action-first ordering on phones.

##### Tested and rejected
* **Hysteresis thresholding** of wire probability: pixel recall 0.80 -> up to 0.89, but tolerant mAP50
  0.0397 -> 0.018-0.028, because neighbouring wires merge.
* **External datasets:** WireSegHR (no licence; generic wire class; only 420 test images released), MPCD (aerial)
  and railway OCS (catenary) do not pass the gates. No fine-tuning was done.

##### Known limitations
* The night gain is measured on a synthetic night condition; the real night photo has no labels.
* YOLO vegetation is unvalidated at night and marks dark sky as vegetation on P-D67F60.
* Dark photos pay two extra WireNet passes.

##### Verified
* 446 tests pass (15 new in `tests/test_v211_field_ui.py`); ruff is clean.
* Headless Chrome (Playwright, `scripts/ui_check.py`) on 5 photos and at phone width: 0 console or page errors;
  all layer toggles, table->canvas and canvas->table selection work; PDFs are valid; no horizontal overflow.
* Test split (one run; `v211_spans_test.jsonl`): clean 0.0204 / 0.0469 and low light 0.0053 / 0.0222, both identical
  to v2.10. Synthetic night: tolerant mAP50 0.0090 -> 0.0313, power recall 0 -> 0.217. Telecom recall rose only
  0.003 -> 0.025 (validation: 0.106).
* `scripts/ui_check.py` re-run after the last UI fixes: all 5 cases pass. Verified in the browser: the vegetation
  toggle is off when disabled; on a phone the order is banner, verdict, viewer, table; there is no focus outline;
  the intake form is centred.

#### v2.10.1: live-server HTTP 500 fix and universal image ingestion (branch `v2.10-hardening`)

**Root cause of the HTTP 500.** Every upload to the live server failed at the first database insert:
`sqlite3.OperationalError: table analyses has no column named submitted_via`. The persistent database in
`var/` predated the dual portals, and `migrate()` only knew one column by name (`vegetation_json`). Tests
and `scripts/server_check.py` always build a fresh database, so neither could see it. The traceback is
kept in `runs/inspection_server_http500.log`.

##### Fixed
* **Schema migration** (`app/core/database.py`, `add_missing_columns`): every ORM column missing from an
  existing SQLite table is added at startup, and existing rows are backfilled from the column's default. A
  future NOT NULL column without a default fails loudly at startup with its name, not on every request.
  The live database was backed up first (`var/backups/wireframe-pre-v210-migration.db`, integrity ok);
  its 4 existing analyses were backfilled with `submitted_via = 'admin'`.
* **Universal ingestion** (`app/pipeline/acquisition.py`):
  - `to_rgb` normalises every Pillow mode to 8-bit RGB:
    - EXIF rotation applied;
    - 16-bit scaled, not clipped;
    - alpha composited over white;
    - CMYK, palette, greyscale and 1-bit converted;
    - multi-frame files use their first frame.
  - BMP and GIF are now accepted.
  - Photos are downscaled to 2048 px on the long edge (`acquisition.max_side_px`). The stored copy is the
    analysed image, so report overlays line up.
  - The upload cap is now 64 MB (was 40).
  - Photos below 320 px or blank are **accepted and flagged**. The reliability grade turns them into
    INCONCLUSIVE - RETAKE PHOTO with the reason. `scripts/batch_inspect.py` still rejects them into
    `rejected.csv` (`reject_low_quality`).
  - Non-images remain a clean 422.
  - Every reader of a stored upload (worker, PDF, admin) uses the same decoder (`load_rgb`).
* **The server now analyses exactly the uploaded pixels.** GPS removal used to re-encode every JPEG
  (`quality="keep"`). That changed pixels slightly, and through the verdict instability of v2.10 it flipped
  a demo pole from HIGH to NOT AT RISK. Metadata is now removed at the JPEG segment level: EXIF is rebuilt
  without GPS, XMP and IPTC are dropped, and the image data is copied byte for byte. The decoded pixels are
  checked to be identical before the copy is used.
* **Strict JSON:** `_jsonable` handles numpy scalars, bools and arrays, NaN and Inf, `Fraction`, `Decimal`,
  bytes and non-string keys. It is applied to every JSON column and to the API result. Unhandled exceptions
  are logged with a traceback and returned as structured JSON.

##### Verified
* 426 tests pass, including 17 new HTTP-upload regression tests (`tests/test_v210_ingestion.py`):
  - every format and mode;
  - EXIF rotation;
  - 64x64, blank and 4000x3000 RGBA photos;
  - non-image input;
  - the exact legacy schema of the live database;
  - JSON sanitising;
  - lossless GPS stripping.
* **Live server over real HTTP** (`scripts/live_upload_check.py`, `docs/thesis/results/live_upload_check_v210.json`):
  4 demo samples, plus 64x64, 4000x3000 RGBA PNG (37.7 MB), greyscale and blank. Every upload returned 202
  and its result and PDF 200, with 0 HTTP 500s.
* The whole-pipeline golden check against v2.9 is still identical on 22/22 validation photos.

#### v2.10: glare gate, output-identical latency rewrite, scoped lint (branch `v2.10-hardening`)

Full methods and tables: `docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.10_hardening.md`. No model was retrained. WireNet
(`ab2353c7`), the span constants and the corroborated-verdict rule are unchanged. `v2.6-baseline`, `7867d56`,
`dc1177b` and `5989657` are untouched. Rules were derived on validation; the test split was used once.

##### Accepted
* **Photometric glare gate** (`quality.glare_gate`): near-white share >= 0.6 and thin-line contrast < 15 L* units
  -> Retake recommended, so NOT AT RISK becomes INCONCLUSIVE and a found hazard is kept with a note.
  - Chosen over an exposure-only gate and a model wire-density gate. On validation it has recall 1.0,
    specificity 0.78 and leave-one-photo-out accuracy 0.86.
  - Validation: silent NOT AT RISK under glare 5 -> 0; clean photos unchanged; 3 of 22 bright-sky control
    photos made INCONCLUSIVE.
  - Test: silent NOT AT RISK under glare 7 -> 3; span-dropout cases 2 -> 0.
* **Latency.** Span assembly no longer scans the full frame per fragment: single-pass label coordinates,
  bounding-box masks, vectorised stitching in the original loop order. Proven output-identical to v2.9
  (whole-pipeline digest, 22/22 validation photos; reference written by v2.9 from a git worktree).
  Dense poles are 35-46% faster in a paired benchmark; the 81-span pole takes 2.3 s on a quiet machine
  (v2.9: 4.58 s).
* **Scoped lint:** `[tool.ruff] extend-exclude` + `force-exclude`; `.agents/`, `.claude/`, virtualenvs and
  data are never linted or fixed.
* `ml/stress.py` `brightsky` control condition (validation only; not in the 8-condition benchmark).
  `scripts/server_check.py --repeat` reports medians and the machine load.

##### Tested and rejected
* **Exposure-only glare gate:** rejects 22 of 22 bright-sky photos. Kept as a documented policy option.
* **Model wire-density gate:** misses 2 of 5 silent cases.
* **Borderline band around the corroboration threshold:** does not stabilise verdicts (41% -> 47-50% changed)
  and gives back up to half of v2.9's NOT AT RISK verdicts.

##### Open, quantified
* **Verdict instability.** With at least 80% of spans retained, mild degradations change the pole verdict in
  41% of validation cases (46 of 111). The motivating glare photo from v2.9 is this case, not span dropout.
  A fix needs a steadier per-span sag measurement.
* **Latency under sustained load.** On this fanless laptop the CPU stages slow by up to 2-3x under sustained
  load and OS background activity. The dense pole measured 4.0-4.9 s in a median-of-3 run at load average
  about 3.8. The < 3.5 s target is met for single uploads on a quiet machine, not guaranteed under load.

#### v2.9: corroborated pole verdicts, demo output, three negative results (branch `v2.9-refinement`)

Full methods and tables: `docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.9_refinements.md`; defense reference:
`docs/thesis/defense_summary_v2.9.md`. No model was retrained. WireNet (SHA-256 prefix `ab2353c7`), the YOLO
detector, `v2.6-baseline`, `7867d56` (v2.7) and `dc1177b` (v2.8) are untouched. Rules and constants were
decided on validation.

##### Accepted
* **Corroborated pole-level sag aggregation** (`hazard.sag_rule: corroborated`). A band sets the verdict only
  when more spans are in it than the per-span test-retest false-alarm rate explains: a one-sided binomial
  test, alpha = 0.05. p0 (high 0.050, medium-or-above 0.090, all scored spans, 492 pairs) was measured on validation by re-measuring each
  photo mirrored. Uncorroborated spans are listed as NOTE lines; the vegetation rules are unchanged.
  Validation verdicts, high / moderate / not at risk: 17 / 3 / 2 -> 2 / 10 / 10; test: 18 / 3 / 0 -> 7 / 7 / 7.
* **Reliability reasons**: every grade below A names the cause (glare, darkness, too few wire pixels,
  camera shake, focus, low light, model uncertainty).
* **Diagnostic figure v2**: grade badge and reason, class and level legends, S/D labels, clearance as
  "~m +/- u", and a strip of verdict reasons. Also in the web screener and the PDF.
* **Motion-coherence measurement** (content-normalised structure tensor). It separates 22/22 synthetic
  motion photos from 22/22 defocused ones. It is used only for the reliability reason.
* **`scripts/server_check.py`**, a repeatable end-to-end check.

##### Tested and rejected (code kept, switched off)
* **Motion-blur router** (no unsharp masking under directional blur): +0.021 power pixel IoU, but span
  tolerant mAP50 0.0177 -> 0.0098-0.0143 on validation motion photos.
* **JPEG deblocking**: tolerant tie, strict 0.0058 -> 0.0043.
* **Service-drop split by chord inclination (> 35 deg)**: a visual audit of 24 validation "drops" found
  pole-to-pole spans seen in perspective. The low-hanging-drop proxy that depends on it is off too.
* **X-junction perfect matching**: identical to the greedy rule with v2.8's tangents (0.0397); worse with
  far tangents (0.0353).

##### Bugs found and fixed
* The blur fallback was coupled to the unsharp decision: routing a photo away from unsharp disabled its
  fallback (`runner._degraded`).
* The blur fallback counted a classified "service drop" as a failed wire (`runner._measured`).
* Steep fragments were classified before the 100 px precision gate.
* The motion measure's padding mask treated any grey pixel near the letterbox value as padding; padding is
  now whole rows and columns.

##### Test-split disclosure
The test split was evaluated more than once in this version. No threshold or rule was chosen from it, and
every superseded run is kept in `docs/thesis/results/superseded/`:
- A first locked run differed slightly from v2.8 (8-condition tolerant mean 0.0355 against 0.0360). Tracing
  it found the drop/fallback coupling bug.
- Validation then exposed the drop misclassification.
- The final segmentation run uses the configuration in this commit and reproduces v2.8 exactly.
- The verdict layer was re-run on test once more, after p0 was re-measured on validation over all scored spans.
  Test verdicts were 6 / 7 / 8 with the earlier subset value, and 7 / 7 / 7 with the final value.
- Server check: 1.34-4.58 s from upload to result; 13 of 14 under 4.5 s (the 81-span pole: 4.58 s). All 14 PDFs
  were valid.

##### Housekeeping
`ruff check --fix .` was run once over the whole working tree. It also reformatted two untracked files
outside the project (`.agents/skills/caveman-compress/scripts/benchmark.py`, `compress.py`): lint-only
changes; both still compile.

#### v2.8: span assembly, hazard interpretation, robustness (branch `v2.8-overnight-sprint`)

Full methods, ablations and limitations are in `docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.8_autonomous_upgrades.md`.
No model was retrained: the v2.7 WireNet weights (SHA-256 prefix `ab2353c7`) and the YOLO detector are
unchanged. Constants were chosen on the validation split; the test split was used once, for the final
tables.

##### Accepted (each measured on validation, and each passing the team's guard of >= 97% of v2.7's
##### power/telecom tolerant pixel IoU)
* Span stitching: collinear gap bridging (70 px, 25 deg) plus the coordinate-descent constants.
  Validation tolerant mAP50 0.0283 (v2.7) -> 0.0397, power spans per label 1.84 -> 1.24.
* Pre-processing: edge-preserving denoise (bilateral filter, triggered by noise or JPEG blockiness);
  the CLAHE darkness trigger turned off (dark-triggered CLAHE cost 0.04 power IoU in low light); flip
  TTA for WireNet. Mean over 8 stress conditions: 0.3788 -> 0.3872.
* CRS measurement-precision gate: chord >= 100 px, derived from sigma / L <= 0.25 delta_lim. Not tuned
  to change verdicts.
* Hazard verdict (AT RISK - HIGH / MODERATE / NOT AT RISK) with plain-English reasons, the team's
  vegetation rules, an approximate clearance with an explicit uncertainty range, and an informational
  congestion index. Chapter III's equations are unchanged.
* Reliability grade A / B / C / Retake recommended from R = 1 - (U + D)/2. Spearman rho 0.64 with
  per-image accuracy; thresholds calibrated on validation.
* Diagnostic three-panel figure embedded in the PDF; batch street-audit CLI with a LabelMe
  review queue; 8-condition stress benchmark script.

##### Tested and rejected
* Multi-scale Retinex: lowered low-light and glare accuracy.
* Multi-scale TTA: +0.006 or less at 2-3.5 s per image.
* Topological loose-end detector: about 10 candidates per photo, precision below 0.03. Shipped
  disabled as a research module.
* Pseudo-label self-training: not attempted, because no candidate set reached the required > 0.75
  precision.

##### Honest findings
* "Always high" is only partly fixed. Fragment noise is removed (high-band spans per split
  90 -> 46 val, 120 -> 75 test), but 17 of 22 val and 18 of 21 test photos still read high. The
  causes are the placeholder delta_lim (high band = sag ratio > 2.4%), a per-photo worst-of-about-30-spans
  rule (P(>= 1 high) is about 0.88), and CRS not separating drop and service wires from spans. These
  were not tuned; they are left for the engineer's calibration (H3) and a team decision on aggregation.
* Glare is unsolved for every variant (power IoU about 0.08); the grade asks for a retake.
* Per-image false positives are largely unlabelled real wires in dense bundles (label
  incompleteness).

##### Verification
* 351 tests passed (294 in v2.7), `ruff check` clean, the field UI's JavaScript parses.
* WireNet weights byte-identical to v2.7 (SHA-256 prefix ab2353c7 before and after).
* Test split, 8 conditions: tolerant mAP50 0.0298 (v2.7) -> 0.0360 (v2.8), strict 0.0196 -> 0.0192 (a tie).
  Regressions: JPEG and glare (strict), and motion blur is still best with enhancement off.
* Server end to end: 16 uploads; latency 1.20-3.45 s (median 2.83 s); all PDFs valid.
  Fixed during the check: unreadable photos with no wires read NOT AT RISK; they now read INCONCLUSIVE - RETAKE PHOTO.

#### v2.7: 1024 px, blur resilience, full-resolution wire segmentation (WireNet)

All figures, tables and limitations are in `docs/thesis/chapter4_v2.7_results.md`; raw results are in
`docs/thesis/results/`. The test split was used once, for the final figures. This section records what
changed in the system and why.

##### Rollback point
* `010d11b` (tag `v2.6-baseline`): the v2.6 code and model, force-added past `.gitignore` at the
  user's request. Nothing has been pushed.

##### Data
* The 145-image span-level export (a superset of the earlier 99) is the only source. It is curated
  to 1024 × 1024 letterboxes ("Fit", padding 114 to match inference). The Roboflow default is black
  padding; 114 is used deliberately so training and inference see the same padding.
* `ml/curate.py`: `--imgsz` letterbox output with polygon transform; external sources default to
  `split: train_only`, so neither they nor any near-duplicate group they belong to can reach val or test.
* External search: no source passed the gate (`ml/sources.yaml: rejected`). The thesis cites no
  Kaggle dataset, and no Kaggle token is present.

##### Blur and low-contrast resilience
* `app/pipeline/enhance.py`: unsharp masking is blur-adaptive — amount 0.6 → 1.5, radius ×1 → ×3 and
  detail threshold ×1 → ×0.25 as the variance of the Laplacian falls below the blur threshold. **Bug
  fixed:** the fixed-parameter version left strongly blurred photos unsharpened (gain 1.0).
  `enhance_strong` is the fallback profile.
* `app/pipeline/runner.py: finalize_detections`: blur fallback on degraded photos. Ultralytics'
  `augment=True` is a no-op for segmentation heads (its test-time augmentation runs only when the head
  is exactly `Detect`), so the fallback adds its own mirrored pass, merged by mask NMS. It is shared
  with `ml.evaluate`, so evaluation measures what the screener does.
* `preprocess(..., model_size)`: the model input (1024) is separate from the VNI gray path (640).
  A test pins that the VNI does not change with the model's input size.
* `ml/train.py`: Albumentations field degradations (motion, defocus and Gaussian blur, CLAHE, flatter
  contrast), set inside the trainer because `model.train()` rejects the argument; perspective and shear
  set to 0. `--resume` keeps the custom trainer, which it previously dropped.
* `ml/degrade.py` and `ml.evaluate --degraded / --no-enhancement / ultralytics`: the degraded test pass.

##### Training on Apple Silicon
* YOLO11s-seg at 1024, batch 2, swapped (7 s → 44 s per step). Batch 1 with Ultralytics' nominal-batch
  accumulation (`nbs` 64) keeps the effective batch at 64 and ran at 1.1 s per step. The MPS
  allocator still crept up, from 4.4 to 6.4 GB by epoch 8; `release_memory_steps` (`empty_cache`
  every 10 steps) kept it flat at 3.4 GB.
* Early stopping ended the first 1024 run on an epoch-1 "best" with 1.3% mask recall. Training was
  continued from epoch 16, and its best checkpoint, global epoch 22, is deployed.

##### Thin-wire masks: diagnosis and the fix
* A perfect 1/4-resolution mask of a 2.1 px wire reaches a median IoU of 0.40, and only 17–21% of
  wire instances can reach 0.5, so YOLO-seg's wire mask AP is capped. SAM 2.1 with the true boxes
  reaches a median IoU of 0.04–0.08 on wires.
* **WireNet** (`ml/wirenet.py`, `app/pipeline/wirenet.py`): a U-Net with a ResNet-34 encoder that
  predicts every pixel at full resolution, trained on wire-dense 512 tiles with cross-entropy + Dice
  + clDice. Best validation pixel IoU: power 0.265 and telecom 0.232 (tolerant 0.525 and 0.418).
* At the operating threshold the 1024 detector proposes almost no wires (test: 0 power and 4 telecom
  spans), so refining its proposals cannot recover wires. Spans are therefore extracted from WireNet's
  class map (`app/pipeline/wire_instances.py`). On test, tolerant mAP50 rises from 0.0199 to 0.0375
  and strict from 0.0198 to 0.0204; v2.6 scores 0.0005.
* Config: `segmentation.wirenet_weights`, `wirenet_instances: skeleton`, `imgsz: 1024`, and
  `conf_min: 0.20`, chosen on the validation split and written with `update_config_values()`. The
  `enhancement.working_long_side` is 1024.

##### Abandoned-wire (Stage 2): attempted, not adopted
* `ml/wirenet_stage2.py` covers label tightening, oversampling, copy-paste, hanging-end synthesis,
  focal loss with class weights, logit adjustment, a power/telecom guard and coverage-F selection.
  Four attempts were made; each either over-predicted abandoned-wire or degraded power and telecom.
  Attempt 3 hit 6/6 validation instances at 0.01 precision, and no inference bias recovered precision.
* The deployed WireNet is Stage 1. The reasons, and what more labelled data is needed, are in Chapter 4
  §4.3.6. All Stage 2 checkpoints are kept in `runs/wireframe/_keep/`.

##### Verification
* **294 tests passed**, `ruff check` clean on app, ml, tests and scripts.
* The end-to-end server check passed: `HybridSegmenter` ready with the four classes; 8 uploads (4
  standard, 4 degraded) completed in 1.4–3.9 s with valid PDFs; enhancement flags present on degraded
  uploads (`docs/thesis/results/server_check_v27.json`).
* NumPy-2 audit regex fixed: it had flagged the `np.ptp()` function, which NumPy 2 keeps.

* Abandoned-wire needs several hundred tightly traced instances. Spans fragment: 466 predicted against
  253 labelled for power. Every per-photo maximum CRS is "high" because of short fragments; scoring
  should require a minimum chord length (not yet implemented). The `conf_min` comment still says
